# Welcome to C360

C360 is the Customer Engagement Platform: the place where a marketing team keeps its contacts, designs emails and texts, sends campaigns and reads what came back. This guide is for the people who sign in to a workspace: administrators who set it up, marketers who run campaigns, and anyone who reads the reports.

A second guide, the Platform Guide, is for the platform team who run the back-office: creating workspaces, plans, features and the platform's own settings. If you were invited to a workspace, you only need this one.

## How the guide is organised

Every page of the app has a chapter, and every chapter has the same shape:

- **What this page is for.** One paragraph on the job the page does.
- **Who can use it.** The permission a role needs, and the feature the workspace needs switched on.
- **The fields and controls.** A table of everything on the page: what each field or button is, and what it changes elsewhere in C360. That is the part to read when a setting is unclear.
- **How to.** The everyday tasks, step by step.
- **What happens behind the scenes.** The business flow: what C360 does with what you entered, and where it shows up later.
- **Things to know.** The limits and the consequences worth remembering.

## Help inside the app

- **The help icon** beside a page's title opens that page's chapter in a panel, so the guide is one click away from the page it describes. Close it with the cross or the Escape key.
- **The small information icon** beside a field opens a note on that one field: what it is and what it affects.
- **The keyboard icon** in the header lists the keyboard shortcuts. Pressing `?` on any page does the same.
- **Grey suggestions.** Some boxes offer a suggestion in grey, with a small **Tab** tag at the right while you are in the box. Press Tab (or the right arrow) while the box is empty to put the suggestion in, then change it or keep it. On a phone or tablet, tap **Use**. Once the box has text, or when it shows an "e.g." example, Tab moves to the next field as usual. Suggestions are offered in Ask for a Report and Change With a Prompt (Insights), the campaign name (from the type, the channel and the month), Subject B of a subject line test (the subject line), and an empty text message (the opt-out line, kept as the last line).
- **The foot of every page** carries the platform's copyright line and a link to the Terms of Use, which also opens from the sign-in page without signing in.

## Three ideas that run through everything

**Workspace.** Your company's own area of C360. Everything you see belongs to one workspace: its users, roles, lists, templates, campaigns and settings. The workspace code, shown as the **Workspace ID** (for example `NWA`), appears on the log-in page, at the top of every page and in every address C360 gives you to register with other systems. Your account belongs to one workspace: the same email address can also have an account in another workspace (a Staging and a Production one, say), with its own password, roles and two-factor.

**Roles and permissions.** What a person may do is set by their role. A role is a set of permissions such as *campaign.edit* or *user.admin*. The chapter for each page names the permission it needs. If a page refuses you, it says which permission to ask an administrator for.

**Features.** Some parts of C360 are switched on per workspace by the platform team: texts, MMS, e-receipts, journeys, A/B testing, API access, the preference centre, barcodes. A page that belongs to a feature that is off is simply not shown, and its chapter says which feature to ask for.

## Logging in, and knowing which workspace you are in

**The log-in page** asks for three things: the **Workspace ID**, your email and your password.

| What you see | What it means |
|---|---|
| *Workspace ID: CITYST* above Email | The address you opened (the workspace's own address, for example `citist.cep.example.com`) already names the workspace. Type only your email and password. |
| A **Workspace ID** field | You opened C360's own address. Type the workspace's ID; under it, **Recent** offers the workspaces you logged in to on this computer: one click fills the field. |
| The button **Log In to CITYST** | The workspace you are about to enter. Each workspace has its own password: Staging's does not open Production. |
| *The workspace, email and password do not match an account. Check the Workspace ID: Staging and Production have different passwords.* | One of the three is wrong. C360 never says whether the address has an account in some other workspace. |

**Inside the app:**

- The **Workspace ID** is at the top right, before the upload, help and notification buttons.
- A **Staging** workspace shows an amber band under the top bar on every page (*Staging workspace · CITYST-STG · sends here are for testing*) and a Staging tag beside the Workspace ID, so nobody mistakes it for Production. Sending engines still send for real: a Staging workspace should send only to test lists.
- The **account menu** (your name at the top right) shows your Workspace ID with Staging or Production, your roles in this workspace and whether two-factor is on. Under it, **Log In to CODE** offers the other workspaces you logged in to on this computer: it opens that workspace's log-in page, where you use that workspace's password.

## Statuses you will meet

| Status | Where | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| Draft | campaigns, lists | Being prepared; nothing has been sent and nothing is live. |
| Test Published | campaigns | Published to test lists only. Production lists cannot be attached. |
| Production Published | campaigns | Published to real people; a scheduled run will go out. |
| Scheduled | campaigns | A production campaign with a run waiting for its time. |
| Active, Sending, Preparing | campaigns | Live right now: a run is building its audience or sending. |
| Completed | campaigns | The run finished. |
| Verified, Not Verified Yet, Verification Failed | senders | Whether a sender address or number has proven it can send. |

## Where to start

A new workspace begins with the chapter **Setting Up a New Workspace**: from the invitation email and the first sign-in through the seven set-up steps to the first production send. **Settings → Set-Up** is the page that walks them, read from the workspace so it always knows what is done. The chapters for the pages it opens come next: Users and Roles, Sending Engines, Suppressions, Contact Lists, Notifications and Approvals.

---

# Home

**Where:** Home in the main navigation, and the C360 mark at the top of the rail.

## What this page is for

Home is the marketing command centre: how the workspace's campaigns are doing, what needs a decision, and the quickest way to start something new. Every figure on it is read live from the same reports and campaigns the other pages show, filtered by the campaigns, channel and period you choose at the top of Marketing Performance. Nothing is entered on Home; it is the place to look first in the morning.

## Who can use it

| | |
|---|---|
| Permission | Everyone who can sign in sees Home. Each card needs the permission of what it reads: the figures, Marketing Performance, Top Campaigns, Governance and the funnel need `report.view`; Needs Attention and Recent Campaigns need `campaign.view`; the set-up card is for administrators (`user.admin`). A card the viewer may not read is left out and the rest close the gap. |
| Features | Active Journeys needs the **journeys** feature; E-Receipts needs **ereceipt**; the SMS and MMS choices in the channel filter need **sms** and **mms**. |

## The fields and controls

### The top of the page

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| The greeting | Good morning, afternoon or evening in the workspace's time zone, with your first name. | Nothing. |
| Create New | Campaign, Template, Contact List, and Journey where journeys are on: one tile each, only the ones you may create. | Opens the Create Campaign pop-up, the New Template page, the contact list wizard. |
| Needs Attention | One card per thing to act on: campaigns with delivery failures in the last week, campaigns waiting for a production publish, campaigns waiting for approval, and **Message Budget** once this month's texts reach the Warn At percent of the budget set under Settings → Message Costs (shown as a percent, never as money); red first, then amber, then the rest; three cards until View All. An opportunity card from the funnel takes one slot when there is one. | Each card's link opens the campaign list filtered to those campaigns. The section is left out when there is nothing to act on. |
| Set Up Your Workspace | For administrators until the workspace is ready: how many of the seven set-up steps are done and what comes next. | Continue Set-Up opens Settings → Set-Up (the chapter Setting Up a New Workspace walks the whole path). Hide Until Later on that page hides the card. |

### Marketing Performance

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Campaigns | A searchable pick of campaigns. Empty means every campaign. | Every card below reads only those campaigns. One campaign picked turns the page into that campaign's own picture. |
| Channel | All Channels, Email, SMS, MMS. | Every card below. Open Rate reads *not tracked* for texts, which have no opens. |
| Period | Last 7, 30 or 90 Days. | Every card below, and the comparison: each figure is shown against the same length of time before it. |
| Delivered | Messages delivered in the period, with the change against the period before. | |
| Open Rate | Opens over delivered, email only; the change in points. | |
| Click Rate | Clicks over delivered; the change in points. | |
| Conversions | Conversions attributed to the campaigns, with the share of clicks that converted. | Reads 0 until attribution is on (Settings → Attribution) and orders arrive. |
| Attributed Revenue | Revenue attributed to the campaigns, against the sum of their revenue targets when they have one. | *Not set up* until attribution is on; Set Up Attribution opens Settings → Attribution for administrators. |
| Campaign ROI | Attributed revenue over campaign cost, with a benchmark word. | *Unavailable* when no campaign in the period has a cost. |

### The cards

| Card | What it shows | Where it leads |
|---|---|---|
| Top and Bottom Campaigns | The campaigns of the period ranked by revenue against target, or by click rate when attribution is not set up; the best and the weakest. | A row opens the campaign. |
| Attributed Revenue vs. Campaign Cost | Revenue as bars and cost as a line per day (per week over a longer period), in total or by campaign, with the target pace when the campaigns have revenue targets. | The Revenue View switch. |
| Engagement Funnel | Delivered, opened, clicked and converted for one channel, with the drop-off between each step. | The channel switch; the funnel feeds the opportunity card in Needs Attention. |
| Channel Snapshot | One line per channel: sent, delivered, the rates. | |
| Next 7 Days | A day picker and the sends scheduled on the day picked, in the campaign's own time zone, journey runs included. | A send opens its campaign. |
| Active Journeys | Each active or paused journey: people in flight, entered, and the conversion; a journey whose last run failed says so. | Opens Journeys. |
| Recent Campaigns | The campaigns changed most recently, with their status and headline figures; sort by Most Recent or Best, filter by status. | A row opens the campaign. |
| Governance | People contacted in the period, and the people held back by reason: frequency cap, no consent, the suppression list; frequency-cap compliance as a bar. | |
| E-Receipts | Receipts sent, their open rate, offer clicks and revenue from receipt offers. The marketing figures above leave receipts out. | |

## How to

**See one campaign's picture.** Pick it under Campaigns. The figures, the revenue chart and the funnel read that campaign alone, and Needs Attention shows only its own alerts.

**Compare this month with last.** Set Period to Last 30 Days. Every figure's change chip is against the 30 days before.

**Act on an alert.** Press the card's link: Review Failures, Publish or Review. The campaign list opens already filtered to those campaigns.

**Start something.** Create New → Campaign opens the Create Campaign pop-up; Template opens New Template; Contact List opens the wizard.

## What happens behind the scenes

1. Home asks for the period's report summary and the one before it, the overview (alerts, revenue and cost, governance, journeys) in one request, the calendar for the week ahead and the recently changed campaigns. The summary and the overview are kept for 30 seconds on the server, so a second look within that time is cheap.
2. A request the viewer may not make hides its card instead of showing an error; a request that fails shows the card with Try Again.
3. The figures are the report's figures: the same numbers as Reports and each campaign's Performance tab, so a number on Home can always be traced to its campaign.

## Things to know

- Home is a reading, not a ledger: figures lag by up to 30 seconds and e-receipts are kept apart from marketing figures.
- Revenue, conversions and ROI come from the orders the shop sends, credited to campaigns every five minutes (see Attribution). Until attribution is on they read zero or *not set up*.
- The page keeps your filters only while you stay on it; the next visit starts at every campaign, all channels, the last 30 days.

---

# Contact Lists

**Where:** Contact Lists in the main navigation; a list's own page (click its name); **Contact Lists → Uploads**; and **Contact Lists → Find a Customer**.

## What this page is for

A contact list is a reusable audience: the people a campaign is sent to. Lists come from a file you upload, from a file of Customer IDs that C360 looks up in your CRM (**Look Up in C360**), from an audience your data team defined in the BI tool, or from a copy of another list. A campaign never reads a list while it sends: when a run starts, the attached lists are merged into the campaign's own frozen copy, so you can change a list at any time without changing a send that was already built.

The page shows every list of the workspace with its size and where it is used, and starts the wizard that creates one.

## Who can use it

| | |
|---|---|
| Permission | `list.view` to see lists and their people; `list.edit` to create, edit, clone, archive and delete; `list.import` to upload a file; `consent.edit` to approve or refuse a list that waits for approval. |
| Features | Always on. A list *for texts* only matters when the workspace has the **sms** feature. |

## The fields and controls

### The page

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Total Lists, Total Contacts, Attached to Live Campaigns | The counts for the workspace: lists, distinct people across them, and lists attached to a campaign that is published to production or sending now. | Nothing; a reading. |
| Search | Filters the table by name as you type. | |
| Type | Production or Test. | A campaign published to production refuses a Test list. Keep real sends and rehearsals apart with it. |
| Source | Upload, BI or Extraction, Cloned. | |
| Find a Customer | Opens the customer search (see *The customer page* below). | |
| Uploads | Opens the Uploads page: every file upload of the workspace with its progress, who started it, Continue, Cancel and its report. | |
| Create List | Opens the wizard. | |
| The table | Name and description; **For** (Email, Texts, Email and Texts); the number of people; the source; the type; how many campaigns it is attached to and how many of those are live; when it changed. A note under the name when the list waits for approval or was refused. | |
| Row menu | **Open** the list, **Clone** it (asks for the copy's name), **Archive** it (**Restore** on an archived list), **Delete** it. | Archive: campaigns can no longer pick the list; Restore makes it active again. Delete is refused while the list is attached to a campaign: archive it instead. Deleting removes its people from the list, never from C360. |

### A list's page

Opened by a list's name. It shows who is in the list, how and when each was added, where the list is used, and the ways to fix it.

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Header | The name and description, the Type (Production or Test), the source, Archived when it is, and where an audience or a copy came from. | |
| Refresh Now (a list that follows an audience) | Brings the list in line with its audience at once, instead of at the hourly refresh, and says how many were added and removed. | People who no longer qualify leave; people who now qualify join; contacts added by hand stay. |
| Clone | Asks for the copy's name and opens the copy. | A new list with the same people today; the two change separately from then on. |
| Edit Details | **List Name** (required, up to 200 characters), **Description**, **List Type** (Production or Test) and **What the List Is For** (Email, Texts, or Email and Texts). | List Type: a Test list is for test sends only, and publishing a campaign to production with it attached is refused. What the List Is For: which column a later upload must have (the address for email, the mobile number for texts, both for both). |
| Add Contacts | **Email Addresses**: one per line, optionally followed by first and last name after commas (`ana@example.com, Ana, Lopez`); up to 500 at a time. | Adds them to this list and says how many were added, new to C360, already in the list, or not valid and skipped. Someone C360 knows is linked to their record; anyone new is added to C360 without a consent answer, so marketing sends leave them out until they opt in. Not offered on an archived list. |
| The note on editing | Says whether the list is attached to live campaigns. | A change here is picked up the next time a campaign builds its Campaign List from it; a Campaign List that was already built does not change. |
| Contacts, Added This Week, Attached Campaigns | The counts. | Nothing; a reading. |
| Attached Campaigns | Each campaign the list is on, with its channel, status, Live, and how many Campaign Lists were already built from it. | The name opens the campaign. |
| Approval | Shown when the workspace's Consent Policy asks for approval before the first send: Awaiting Approval, Approved or Refused, with who decided, when and their note. Someone with `consent.edit` writes an optional **Note** (up to 300 characters, kept on the compliance record) and presses **Approve List** or **Refuse**. | Awaiting: campaigns can be built with the list, but none sends to it. Approved: campaigns can send to it. Refused: no campaign sends to it until it is approved. |
| Compliance Record | The source, the consent basis, where it was collected, who attested and when, the approval and the consent policy today; **Consent today** per channel (opted in from the file, confirmed since, no consent on record, unsubscribed). **Show Details** adds the attestation wording and every upload with its figures. | Nothing; the record. The consent figures are rebuilt each time the page opens; the attestation and the approval never change. |
| Governance Check · Marketing Email, as of Today | How many of the list's people a marketing email could reach today, with the reasons the others cannot. | Nothing; a reading, with the send engine's own rules. Transactional campaigns ignore consent, but not blocked or invalid addresses. |
| Search contacts, Consent | Filter the people by name, email address or customer id, and by their marketing-email verdict: Can Receive, Cannot Receive, Unsubscribed, No Consent on Record, Bounced, Complained, Blocked, Invalid Address, Suppressed. | The table below. |
| The people | 50 a page: Email (opens the customer's page when C360 knows them), First Name, Last Name, Added On, Source (Upload, Manual, Audience, Cloned, Rule) and Marketing Email (Can Receive, or why not). A box per row, and one for the whole page. | |
| Export All / Export Selected | A CSV of everyone in the list, or of the ticked people: email, first name, last name, mobile, the day added, the source and the customer id. | Nothing changes. Cells that a spreadsheet would read as a formula are made safe. |
| Remove from List | Takes the ticked people out, after asking. Not offered on an archived list. | They leave this list only; they stay in C360 and in every other list. On a list that follows an audience, someone who still qualifies comes back at the next refresh. |

### The wizard: Upload a File

| Step | Field or control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|---|
| New Contact List | Upload a File / Look Up in C360 / BI or Extraction Tool / Clone a List | Where the people come from. Look Up in C360 is offered only when a Report Database is ticked *Use for List Generation* (see *The wizard: Look Up in C360* below). | The steps that follow. |
| Add Your File | List Name | The list's name, up to 200 characters. | How it is listed everywhere, including a campaign's Send To table. |
| | Description | Optional. Shown under the name in the table. | |
| | What the List Is For | Email, Texts, or Email and Texts. | Which column the file must have (an email address, a mobile number, or both), how a new person is recognised, and how each row is judged (email consent and address status, or text consent and the number's standing). Changed later on the list's Edit Details. |
| | Type | Production or Test. | See Type above. |
| | CSV File | The file: CSV only, up to 50 MB and 250,000 rows, comma, semicolon, tab or pipe separated, UTF-8 or Windows-1252. **Download Sample CSV** gives a file with the recognised columns in the right shape. | The file is stored privately, read in the background and deleted as soon as its rows are staged. |
| Match Your Columns | One choice per column of the file | What each column means: Email, Mobile number, First name, Last name, Postal code, Loyalty ID, Loyalty tier, Last order date, Time zone, Email consent date, Text consent date, or *keep as a custom value*. C360 guesses from the headers; confirm or correct. | The required column must be matched. A consent date column becomes an opt-in for people C360 has no answer for. Any other column is kept on the person's list entry under its header in lower case, spaces as underscores (*Store Name* becomes `store_name`), up to 50 columns: a tag of that name finds it at send (coupon columns feed the barcode and QR code blocks). Time zone is the person's own, an IANA name such as America/Chicago; journeys that wait until a time of day use it, and a name that is not recognised is left out. |
| Tag Values | Template (from a campaign) or Check Against a Template (optional) | The template whose tags the list is for. Opened from a campaign's Add Contact List it is the campaign's template; otherwise choose one, or **Skip**. | Without a template nothing changes: the file's other columns are kept as above. |
| | Value From, for each tag | **File Column** (and which column), **Customer Attribute** (Settings → Customer Attributes), **Customer Record** (read at send, as today) or **Default Value** (the same words for everyone). C360 suggests one: a column of the tag's name, an attribute the tag reads, the customer record for names. | The values are kept with each list entry under the tag's key, where the send looks first: what you review is what is sent. An attribute whose source is a Report Database is read for the file's people while the file is checked, and kept with each customer too. |
| Where Did These People Agree? | Consent Basis | Signed up on our website or app; Enrolled in store or in the loyalty programme; Asked us directly; Existing customers under implied consent. A bought or rented list is not allowed. | Recorded on every consent written from the file and on the list's compliance record. A Texts or Email-and-Texts list cannot use implied consent: marketing texts need an express opt-in. |
| | Where the Consent Was Collected | Optional, up to 300 characters: a form name, a store, a campaign. | Kept as the evidence, so the proof can be found later. |
| | The attestation | A sentence the uploader confirms in their own name, worded by the workspace (Settings → Consent Policy). | The import cannot be applied without it. Who confirmed it, and when, is kept. |
| Review Before Importing | The report | How many rows will be imported and how many are held back (no address, not a valid address, the same address earlier in the file, and under a strict policy, no consent date); how many people are new to C360 and how many it already knows; how the imported people would be judged by a marketing send today: can receive, unsubscribed, bounced, complained, blocked, invalid, suppressed, no consent on record; and with a template, **Tag Values**: for each tag, how many of the people have a value and the first one. People without a value get the tag's default at send. | Nothing yet. **Import** starts the import; **Continue in the background** closes the wizard and leaves it running. |
| List Created | Where the list is, and what to do next. | | |

### The wizard: Look Up in C360

For a list you have as IDs only: the file holds one Customer ID (or Lead, Account or Contact ID) per row, and C360 reads each person from your CRM: name, mobile number, email, postal code, loyalty ID, time zone and whether they may be contacted. The CRM is one of the workspace's Report Databases ticked **Use for List Generation** (Settings → Report Databases); it is only ever read. Six steps: List Details, the IDs, Tag Values, Consent, Review, Done. There are no columns to match.

| Step | Field or control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|---|
| List Details | Look Up in C360 | The card beside Upload a File. Shown only when at least one database is ticked for list generation. | Opens the two choices below on the same step. |
| | Look Up In | Shown when more than one database is offered: tick one or more. They are searched in the order ticked (the number beside each says its turn); the first that has the ID supplies the person. With one database, it is used without asking. | Where each ID is read from. |
| | Role | Customer, Lead, Account, Account Primary Contact, or Any Role (Party ID). | Only people who hold the role in the CRM are imported; the others are held back as *Not a Customer* (and so on). It also names the IDs everywhere in the wizard: Customer ID, Lead ID, Account ID, Contact ID, or Party ID for any role. |
| | List Name, What the List Is For, List Type, Description | As for Upload a File. | What the List Is For decides the rules below: a texts list needs a usable mobile number, an email list a usable address, both lists both. |
| Customer IDs (the step is named after the role) | *Add your Customer IDs. C360 looks each one up and brings the rest of the person with it.* | The file: CSV only, up to 50 MB and 2,000,000 IDs, a header row and one ID per row. The ID column is the only column, or one named Customer ID, Party ID, Account ID, Lead ID or Contact ID; other columns are kept with each list entry, as for Upload a File, and can fill a template's tags. **Download Sample CSV** gives a one-column file with the right header. | The file is stored privately and read in the background; the lookup starts as soon as it is read. |
| Tag Values | As for Upload a File: a template (optional; **Skip** without one) and, for each of its tags, a column of the file, a customer attribute, the customer record or the default. | Answered while the lookup runs, and saved at once. A customer attribute that reads a Report Database is read for the people found, by their ID. Answered or changed after the rows were checked, the values are read on their own (*Reading the tag values*) without checking the file again; importing waits for it. The Review step shows how many people have a value for each tag. |
| Consent | As for Upload a File. | The consent dates come from C360: *Allow Solicitation* Y on the number or address is an opt-in, dated by the solicitation change date (or the day it was linked), for people C360 has no answer for. | You can answer while the lookup runs. |
| Review | **Looked Up in C360**, first | The databases and the role, how many IDs in how long; **Found in C360**; each reason rows were **Held Back**, with its count; each **Warning**. Then **Filled From C360**: how many of the usable people came with a name, mobile number, email, loyalty ID, postal code, time zone, text consent and email consent. | Nothing yet. The usual review follows (new, existing, warnings, errors, the governance check) and **Create List** imports. |

Why a row is held back (for a texts list; an email list checks the email address the same way):

| Reason | What C360 found |
|---|---|
| *Customer ID not found* | No person with that ID in any of the databases. |
| *No Customer ID in this row* | The ID cell is empty. |
| *Same ID as line N* | The ID appears earlier in the file. |
| *Disabled in the source database* | The party is disabled in the CRM. |
| *Not a Customer* (or the role chosen) | The party does not hold the role. |
| *No primary phone* | No current primary phone in the CRM. |
| *Number no longer in use* | The primary phone's end date has passed. |
| *Number not valid* | Marked not valid in the CRM, or not 10 digits. |
| *On Do Not Disturb* | The number is on DND. |
| *Phone not open to solicitation* | Allow Solicitation is N, and the party is not a Customer. |
| *No email*, *Email not valid*, *Email not open to solicitation* | The same checks on the primary email, for an email list. |

A **Customer** whose number is not open to solicitation is still imported, with the warning *Customer not open to solicitation: imported with no text consent*: transactional texts reach them, marketing texts hold them back.

The other two sources are shorter: **BI or Extraction Tool** asks for the audience (the list then follows the audience: who leaves is removed and who joins is added, hourly and on *Refresh now*), **Clone a List** asks for the list to copy and the copy's name.

### The Uploads page

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| The table | Newest first: **List and File** (the list, the file and its rows; for a lookup, its IDs and *Looked up in* the databases), **Status** (In Progress, Waiting for You, Imported, Failed, Cancelled), **Progress** (what the upload is doing, with a bar while it works, and under it the count and the time left: *1,234,000 of 1,320,000 Customer IDs looked up · about 6 min left*) and **Uploaded** (when, and who). | |
| Continue | On an upload waiting for you (matching, consent, review). Needs `list.import` or `list.edit`. | Opens the wizard again at the step it waits on. |
| Report | On any upload that was checked: the review figures, and **Download the Row-by-Row Report**, a CSV with every line of the file, whether it was imported or held back and why, how the person was recognised, and their marketing email status. | Nothing changes. |
| Open the List | On an imported upload. | Opens the list's page. |
| Cancel | On an upload that is not finished and not already adding people. Needs `list.import` or `list.edit`. | The file and the staged rows are discarded. An import that is already adding people finishes. |

The upload button in the header shows the uploads running on every page, with the same count and time left, and raises an alert when one finishes, fails, or waits for a review. A large upload (1,000,000 rows or more unless the platform team set another size) also emails the person who started it when it has been checked, when it is imported and if it fails, so they can leave it running and come back. The time left is worked out on the page from the progress it sees: it shows after a few seconds of each step, and starts again when the step changes (reading, looking up, checking, adding).

## How to

**Upload a file.** Contact Lists → Create List → Upload a File → name the list, say what it is for, choose Production or Test, add the file. Match the columns and confirm. Say where the people agreed and confirm the attestation. Read the review, then Import. The list is a draft until the import is applied; leaving the wizard before that deletes the draft.

**Make a list from Customer IDs.** Contact Lists → Create List → Look Up in C360 → tick the databases (when there are several) and choose the role → name the list, say what it is for, choose Production or Test → Next → add the file of IDs. Answer the consent questions while C360 looks the IDs up. Read **Looked Up in C360** at the top of the review, then Create List. A large file can be left running: Continue in the Background, and come back from the Uploads page or the email.

**Check a lookup before a big one.** Try a file of 20 IDs first and compare what *Filled From C360* and the held-back reasons say with the CRM; then send the full file.

**Prepare a file that imports cleanly.** Use Download Sample CSV as the model: a header row, one person per row, dates as `YYYY-MM-DD`, mobile numbers with the country code (`+14045550101`). Put an email consent date and a text consent date in for everyone who gave one: without a date, a new person has no consent on record and marketing sends leave them out.

**Keep a do-not-contact list first.** Before the first upload, import the people who opted out elsewhere under Settings → Suppressions. Then no upload can ever reach them.

**Approve a list that waits.** When the workspace's Consent Policy asks for approval before the first send, a fresh upload sets its list to *awaiting approval* and every campaign holds its people back as *in a list awaiting approval*. Someone with `consent.edit` opens the list and presses Approve, or Refuse with a note.

**Reuse a list on several campaigns.** Attach it in each campaign's Send To step. The list's page names every campaign it is on, and warns before an edit when one of them is live.

## What happens behind the scenes

1. **The file is stored and queued**, never processed inside your request. Reading, checking and importing run in the background, in chunks, at most a few uploads at a time across the whole platform and never two of the same workspace at once, so one large file cannot slow everyone down. The page keeps showing progress; a hidden browser tab stops asking.
2. **Reading** stages every row and guesses the columns from the headers.
3. **Checking** holds back rows without a usable address, rows whose address appears earlier in the file, and, under a strict policy, rows without a consent date. Everyone else is matched to a person C360 knows (by loyalty ID first, then by address or number) or marked as new. Each importable row is judged exactly as a marketing send would judge that person today, with the send engine's own rules, so a word means the same at upload and in a campaign's results.
4. **The declaration** is recorded: the basis, where it was collected, who attested and when. With a template, the **Tag Values** are read for the rows that can be imported: a file column as it is, a database attribute from its source (by the person's ID there: the file's Party ID column, or the ID C360 keeps), and counted.
For a file of IDs (Look Up in C360), a **lookup** comes between reading and checking: the IDs are read from the CRM 1,000 at a time, on a read-only connection, one upload at a time per workspace, so the CRM barely notices. A worker that stops carries on from the last batch it finished. The ID then becomes the person's key in C360: someone C360 already has under that ID is recognised by it first, a new person is created under it, and someone recognised by address or number gets the ID added, so the next file finds the same people.

5. **Importing** creates the new people, adds everyone with a usable address to the list (whatever their status: people resubscribe, addresses get unblocked, and the send engine decides again for real at every run), writes an opt-in for a consent date only where C360 had no answer, and never overrides an unsubscribe. The list becomes active; under an approval policy it waits for approval first.
6. **The compliance record** on the list's page shows, for as long as the list exists: the source, every upload with its declaration, the approval, the policy as it stands today and today's consent by channel.

## Things to know

- Uploading someone does not give consent. Only a consent date in the file, a sign-up through a C360 form or a preference page, or a record from your other systems does.
- The review's numbers add up: rows in the file = held back + ready; ready = added + already in the list. The row-by-row report is the audit trail of an upload.
- Lists are never deleted while a campaign uses them; archive instead. People are never deleted from C360 by a list action.
- A Test list cannot go to production. Make the real list a Production one from the start, or clone it.
- Excel files are not accepted; save as CSV first.
- A row whose Party ID cell is empty is recognised by its address (or number) instead, like a file without the column.
- Look Up in C360 never writes to the CRM: it only reads, with the account the Report Database connects as.
- A lookup takes about a second or two per 1,000 IDs, depending on the network to the CRM; 1.3 million IDs take a few tens of minutes. The Uploads page shows the time left.

## The customer page

**Where:** **Find a Customer** on the Contact Lists page (`/customers`); a customer's name on a journey's Customers card, on Settings → Events and on a list's people opens their page directly.

One customer across the workspace, read from the records C360 syncs (or an upload added). Nothing is edited here: the record is C360's.

| Part | What it shows |
|---|---|
| Header | The name, the customer id, where the record came from and when it was last updated, and a tag per channel saying whether they can receive marketing (Email: Can receive; SMS: No consent). |
| Record | Email addresses, phone numbers and the address, as the record holds them. |
| Consent | Per channel: the consent status, whether they can receive and why not, when they opted in or out and how, and any suppression. This is the verdict the send engine uses. |
| Engagement | What Unengaged Contacts (Settings → Governance) sees for this person. **Last Engaged**: their last click, purchase or preference visit ("Never, in the kept history" when there is none). **Marketing Email**: Gets marketing email; a tag when they are left out (**Unengaged since 3 March 2027**), until they click, buy or visit the preference page; or No Unengaged Contacts rule applies. **Emails, 180 Days**: the marketing emails sent to them in the rule's period. |
| Custom Attributes | Every field C360 sent with the record (loyalty tier, home store, time zone, dates), which conditions, splits, date entries and time-of-day waits read. |
| Journeys | Every journey they entered, with where they are now, whether they converted and **View Timeline**, which opens their timeline on the journey's page; and the journeys they are kept out of by a suppression. |
| Events | The last 50 events received for them, with their data. |
| Orders | The last 20 orders Attribution received, and the campaign each was credited to. |
| Messages | The last 30 messages campaigns and journeys sent them, with what became of each (delivered, opened, clicked, or why it was not sent). |
| Contact Lists | The lists they are on and how they got there. Lists a journey makes for its own sends are left out. |

**Finding a customer.** Type part of a name, an email address, a mobile number (digits only are fine) or a customer id in **Search customers**; the results follow as you type, 25 a page, with the Customer, Email, Mobile, Customer ID, Source and when the record was Updated. The search stays in the page's address, so a link or the browser's Back returns to it. Click a name to open the customer. A customer who is not in the records does not appear: C360 has to sync them, or an upload has to add them, before C360 can reach them. On a customer's page, **Find Another Customer** goes back to the search; nothing on the page changes the record.

Who may see it: anyone who may see contact lists, campaigns or journeys.

# Campaigns

**Where:** Campaigns in the main navigation; a campaign's own page at Campaigns → the campaign.

## What this page is for

A campaign is what is sent, to whom, when and why. Every actual send is a run of its campaign against a copy of its lists frozen when the run starts, so a recurring or drip campaign stays one campaign with many runs and its results roll up. The Campaigns page lists and plans them, as a table or a calendar; a campaign's page shows everything about one campaign: its status and the actions it allows, its performance, its audience, its content, and what every recipient's message became.

Creating and editing a campaign is the Campaign Set-Up, which has its own chapter.

## Who can use it

| | |
|---|---|
| Permission | `campaign.view` to see campaigns and their results; `campaign.edit` to create, edit, clone and archive; `campaign.publish` to publish to production, pause, resume and cancel a schedule; `campaign.approve` to approve a campaign when the workspace asks for approvals. |
| Features | Texts need **sms** (and **mms** for picture messages), e-receipt campaigns need **ereceipt**, subject line tests need **ab_testing**, journey-generated campaigns need **journeys**. |

## The fields and controls

### The Campaigns page

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Create Campaign | Opens the Create Campaign pop-up (see Campaign Set-Up). | |
| Upcoming campaigns | The next four scheduled sends: channel, name, the date and the time in the campaign's own time zone, recipients. | A send opens its campaign. |
| List View / Calendar View | The table, or the month with every campaign as a bar over the days it runs. | Remembered in the address, so a link keeps the view. |
| Saved views | All, Mine, Sending Today, Needs Attention, Awaiting Approval (shown to approvers and publishers), Drafts. | One click sets the filters below to that question. |
| Search | By name or code. | |
| Filters | Triggered By (manual, journey, and the owner), Status, Type, Channel, Date Created (a preset or a custom range), Tag. | The table and the calendar alike. In the calendar the month replaces the date range. |
| The table | Campaign Name (with Continue Setup for an unfinished draft), Type, Channel, Status, Triggered By, Audience, Next Send (the day and the time in the campaign's zone; *local time* when the send follows each recipient's clock), Created. Sort by the column headers. A drip adds a line under its name ("2 of 4 messages sent", "5 messages · starts Mon 26 Oct", "Stopped after 2 of 5", "3 messages · draft") and its Next Send names the message ("09:00 CT · Message 3 of 4"; "On hold" while paused). | |
| Row actions | Pause, Resume or Approve beside the menu when the status allows; the menu: Continue Setup or Edit, View Journey for a journey campaign, View Results, Clone, Pause, Resume, Cancel Schedule, Stop Remaining Messages (a running or paused drip; it asks first), Archive, Unarchive. | Each action is only offered when the campaign's status allows it and you have the permission. |
| Select | The checkbox per row, or every campaign on the page. | Shows the bulk bar: Clone, Pause, Resume, Archive for the selection. Campaigns that cannot take the action are skipped and named. |
| Rows per Page, Previous, Next | Pages of 50 by default. | |
| The calendar | Monday first, previous and next month, Today; a bar per campaign coloured by status (green going out or sent, amber paused or test published, red failed or cancelled, grey a draft that plans a date); more than three on a day fold into *+n more*. A drip is one bar with a numbered marker on each send day (filled once sent, outlined to come; hover for the message and time); a paused drip's bar stops at its last send and says On hold. The week view and the day list show each drip message on its own. | A bar opens the campaign. |

### A campaign's page

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| The header | The name, its status, channel and type chips, Manual or Journey, the code (copies on click), the description. | |
| The main button | What the campaign most needs next: Continue Setup for an unfinished draft, Production Publish once a test has been sent, Resume when paused. | Opens the set-up at the right step, or acts at once. |
| Clone, More Actions | Clone makes "Copy of …" with the settings, lists, exclusions and targets, never the runs or results. For a drip it asks first for the copy's name and start date, copies every message with its own template copy, and opens the copy's Drip Campaign Setup step. More Actions: Edit Campaign, Send a Test, Production Publish, Pause, Cancel Schedule, Archive, Unarchive, View Results, as the status allows. | |
| Overview | The headline figures (audience, sent, delivery rate, opens and clicks or replies and STOPs, conversions), the campaign's facts (purpose, owner, template, sender, tags), when and how it goes out, its dates, its targets, and a preview of the message. A published drip shows the Series Timeline instead: each message with its state and figures, Skipped with the reasons and View People, a calendar, and the banners for scheduled, paused (who and when), stopped early and a failed message (Retry Message when nobody got it). | Edit on a card opens the set-up at the step that owns it. |
| Performance | Sent, delivered, opened, clicked, bounced, unsubscribed, complaints and conversions per run, the curve by hour or by day, and for an email campaign with a subject line test, A and B side by side with the winner and Send A / Send B to the Rest while people wait. | |
| Recipients | One row per person of a run: address or number, name, status (Queued, Sent, Delivered, Opened, Clicked, Bounced, Marked as Spam, Failed), the reason, the times, the engine's message id; filter by status, search, Export CSV. A row opens the message's timeline with every callback the engine sent, and Check with the Engine asks Postal directly. Blocked & Unsubscribed lists this campaign's people who cannot be sent to and why. | |
| Audience | The lists attached and excluded, the funnel from everyone in the lists to the estimated recipients, the reasons people are left out, system exclusions, and once sent, the frozen picture of the last send beside today's estimate; Export CSV of the frozen list with who was left out and why. | |
| Content | The template, subject line and preheader (the campaign's own or the template's), the sender, the length of a text, the receipt layout of an e-receipt, with a desktop and mobile preview. **Open in the Designer** (or Message Editor) opens the template in its own tab. The editor shows **Back to Campaign** at the top and **Save & Return to Campaign**: it saves your changes, closes the editor's tab and brings the campaign back in front, its preview already showing the new version. | The preview here refreshes by itself whenever the template is saved in another tab. |
| Activity Log | Who did what and what the engine did, newest first, with a filter for warnings and errors. Entries are never changed. | |

### Statuses and what they allow

| Status | Meaning | Actions |
|---|---|---|
| Draft | Being prepared. An unfinished set-up has no page yet: it opens at the step it stopped on. | Edit, Send a Test, Clone, Archive; Production Publish |
| Test Published | A test was sent to colleagues; nothing has gone to real people. | The same |
| Production Published | Published to real people. | Pause, Clone, Archive, View Results |
| Scheduled | Production published with a run waiting for its time. | Cancel Schedule, Clone, Archive |
| Active, Sending, Preparing | A run is building its audience or sending now. | Pause |
| Paused | A send stopped in progress: messages already handed to the engine still go, queued ones wait; runs that come due wait. | Resume |
| Completed | The run finished. | Clone, Archive, View Results |
| Cancelled | The schedule was cancelled. | Clone, Archive |
| Archived | Out of the everyday list, kept for reporting. Archive replaces delete. | Unarchive, Clone |

## How to

**Find what needs you.** The Needs Attention saved view lists failed, waiting and unapproved campaigns; Awaiting Approval lists what approvers must decide; Drafts lists what is unfinished.

**Pause a send that is going wrong.** Pause on the row or the page. Messages the engine already has still go out; everything queued waits until Resume.

**Stop a scheduled send.** Cancel Schedule. The campaign returns to an editable draft.

**Reuse a campaign.** Clone. Rename the copy, check its lists and schedule, publish.

**Pause, resume or stop a drip.** Pause holds every waiting message. Resume shows first what happens to the messages whose day passed while paused (they go at the next send time, one a day, and later ones move only if they must). Stop Remaining Messages cancels the rest for good; the drip is Completed, stopped early, and its templates are free again. To send it again, clone it.

**Retry a drip message that failed.** When a message failed and nobody received it, its card and banner offer Retry Message: it goes again now to the same audience.

**Approve a campaign.** When the workspace's Approvals setting is on, a campaign asks for approval on its Review & Publish step; an approver sees it under Awaiting Approval, opens it and approves or sends it back with a note. The person who asked can never be the one who approves.

**Read what happened to one person's message.** Recipients → search the address → open the row. The timeline shows when it was queued, handed to the engine, and every delivery, bounce, open and click the engine reported; Check with the Engine fetches the engine's own record.

## What happens behind the scenes

1. **Publishing** runs every check (a published template with content, an opt-out link in a marketing email, a verified sender, at least one production list, a time in the future for a schedule) and creates the run or runs: one for a one-time send, one per occurrence for a recurrence as they come due, one per step for a drip series.
2. **At the run's time** the dispatcher, which looks every minute, starts the run: the included lists are merged into the campaign's own frozen list, one entry per person, the excluded lists' people marked, and governance applied in order: bounced, complained or blocked addresses, invalid addresses, the suppression list, unsubscribes, consent, the campaign's topic, unengaged contacts (marketing email only, unless the campaign includes them; Settings → Governance), frequency limits, duplicates. Transactional, service and legal campaigns skip the consent rules but never a blocked or invalid address.
3. **Sending** hands the messages to the sender's engine in batches; a subject line test splits a sample between A and B and holds the rest until the winner is chosen; recipient-local sending holds each message until the chosen time in the person's own zone.
4. **The engine calls back** and every message's row moves forward: delivered, opened, clicked, bounced, complained. The campaign's figures, the report and Home all read those rows.
5. **The activity log** records each step, and a failed run raises a banner on the campaign and an alert on Home.

## What a text campaign cost

On the Performance tab, people with View Message Costs see a **Cost** card for an SMS, MMS or WhatsApp campaign: the actual figure from the parts each message really used (WhatsApp: from Meta's delivery reports, so undelivered and free messages cost nothing), next to the estimate made at publish. While it is still sending it shows the figure so far. Economics then compares the Planned Campaign Cost with this actual figure.

## Things to know

- **Unengaged** as the reason someone was left out means the workspace's Unengaged Contacts rule: no click, purchase or preference visit in its period, after at least its number of marketing emails. The person is not unsubscribed; their next click or purchase brings them back. A win-back campaign reaches them with Include Unengaged Contacts.

- The frozen list is made when a run starts, not at publish: a scheduled send uses the lists as they are on the day, and governance is always decided at send time.
- A Test list can never go to production; a test publish never counts in results.
- Pause does not recall messages the engine already accepted.
- A journey-generated campaign is read-only here; change the journey instead.
- The hourly rate limit is stored and shown but not enforced yet; the engine's Messages per Minute is.

---

# Reports

**Where:** Reports, in the main menu.

## What this page is for

Reports shows how the workspace performed over a period, from the figures C360 already records: sends, deliveries, opens, clicks, bounces, complaints, unsubscribes and attributed revenue. Nothing is collected here that the campaigns and journeys do not already keep. Two datasets share the page: **Campaigns** (the default) and **Journeys**.

## Who can use it

Anyone whose role carries Reports · View (`report.view`). Scheduling the weekly email needs User Admin (`user.admin`). The Journeys dataset is offered when the workspace has the Journeys feature.

## The fields and controls

| Control | What it does |
|---|---|
| Dataset | **Campaigns** or **Journeys**. The period applies to both; the other controls change with the dataset. |
| Period | This Month, Last 7 Days, Last Month, or Custom (a start and an end date, in the workspace's time zone). |
| Channel (Campaigns) | All Channels, Email or SMS. |
| Journey (Journeys) | Every Journey, or one journey of the workspace. |
| Export CSV | The dataset being shown as a file: the campaigns of the period, or every journey's every day. |
| Schedule Report (Campaigns, administrators) | The weekly email: the day, the hour and who receives it. |

### The Campaigns dataset

Four tiles (Sent, Delivered, Opened, Clicked, each with its rate and a note), **Sent and Opened by Day** (Sent and Delivered for SMS), **Best and Worst** (the highest open rate, the highest click rate, the most unsubscribes, the best send hour, over campaigns with at least 50 sends) and **Campaigns in the Period**, one row per campaign that sent anything, with a checkbox to pick two to six and **Compare** them side by side.

### The Journeys dataset

| Part | What it shows |
|---|---|
| Entered | Customers who entered a journey in the period, and how many journeys had any activity. |
| Converted | Customers whose goal was reached in the period (or, in a journey without a Goal step, who reached an Exit), the conversion rate over those entered, and the attributed revenue. |
| Messages Sent | The journeys' messages sent in the period, with the delivered rate and how many a step held back (consent, suppressions, caps, quiet hours) or failed. |
| Opened | The open rate over those delivered, and the click rate. |
| Entered and Converted by Day | The daily curve for the period. |
| Journeys in the Period | One row per journey: its status, entered, converted (with the rate), revenue, sent, delivered, open and click rates, held back and failed. The name opens the journey's page. |

The CSV has a finer grain than the table: one row per journey per day, with every figure above, and a total line.

## How to

1. **Read a month.** Leave Period on This Month; switch Dataset to Journeys to see the journeys' side of the same month.
2. **Follow one journey.** On the Journeys dataset choose it under Journey; every tile, the curve and the CSV narrow to it.
3. **Keep the numbers.** Export CSV while the period and the filter are set; the file is named for the dataset and the period.

## What happens behind the scenes

The Campaigns dataset reads the execution tables once per channel for the period and keeps the answer for 30 seconds. The Journeys dataset reads enrollments (entered by the day they entered, converted by the day the goal was reached, with their revenue), the journeys' generated campaigns' messages (test runs left out) and the message steps that were held back or failed, in the workspace's time zone, and keeps the answer for 30 seconds as well. A journey deleted since stays out of the figures.

## Things to know

- Rates over fewer than 50 sends are left out of Best and Worst.
- The Journeys dataset counts a message on the day it was sent, an entry on the day the customer entered, and a conversion on the day the goal event arrived, so the three can fall on different days for the same customer.
- Held back is the message steps governance stopped (no consent, a suppression, a frequency cap, quiet hours); the journey page's Customers card can list the customers behind each reason.
- The weekly email carries the Campaigns dataset.

---

# Campaign Set-Up

**Where:** Campaigns → **Create Campaign** (also on Home) opens the Create Campaign pop-up; then the five steps (four for a drip: see A Drip Campaign below). On a campaign's page, **Continue Setup** or **Edit Campaign** opens the same steps. Inside the steps, the pencil beside the campaign's name (**Edit Details**) reopens the pop-up, and **Save and Close** keeps the draft and leaves.

## What this page is for

The set-up is where a campaign is defined: who it is for, what it says, a test, the targets it is measured against, and when and how it goes out. It starts with the Create Campaign pop-up (the basics), then five steps: Audience, Content, Test, Targets, Review & Publish. Continue checks the step you are on and saves, so a draft can be left at any step and picked up where it stopped. Nothing is sent to customers until Publish to Production.

## Who can use it

| | |
|---|---|
| Permission | `campaign.edit` for the set-up; `campaign.publish` for the publish itself; `campaign.approve` to approve when the workspace asks for approvals. Someone without `campaign.edit` sees the campaign page with a note that the set-up is unfinished. Create a Journey Instead needs `journey.edit`; Add Contact List's Upload New and Audience or Clone need `list.edit`; New Template needs `template.edit`; New Sender needs `provider.edit`. |
| Features | A channel is offered only when the workspace has its feature: **email**, **sms**, **mms**, **whatsapp**. Multiple Channels needs at least two of email, SMS and MMS. Create a Journey Instead needs **journeys**. |

## The fields and controls

### Create Campaign (the basics)

The same pop-up opens as **Edit Details** for a saved campaign, with **Save** in place of **Create Campaign**.

| Field | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Campaign Name | Required, up to 200 characters, unique among the workspace's live campaigns. Internal: your team sees it, customers never do. | How the campaign is listed everywhere: the Campaigns list, reports and Home. |
| Campaign Type | **Regular Marketing** (one-time or scheduled; marked Most Common), **Drip** (several messages on a set schedule), **E-Receipt** (sent after a purchase), **Other** (anything the others don't cover). Choosing Drip shows when a journey suits better, with **Create a Journey Instead**, which opens Create Journey with the name and description you typed. | Which steps follow: a drip has Audience, Drip Campaign Setup, Targets and Test & Publish; an e-receipt has no Audience step and no schedule, because the point of sale triggers each send. It also sets Message Purpose until you choose one yourself. |
| Channel | **Email**, **SMS** (text up to 160 characters), **MMS** (text with an image), **WhatsApp** (a template Meta approved), **Multiple Channels**. A drip cannot use Multiple Channels or WhatsApp; an e-receipt is Email, or SMS for registers that ask for a text receipt. | Which templates and senders the Content step offers, how the audience is judged (email consent and addresses, or text consent and numbers), and what the message can carry. |
| Message Purpose | **Marketing**, **Transactional**, **Service** or **Legal**. Until you pick, it follows the type: Regular Marketing and Drip are Marketing, E-Receipt is Transactional (and cannot be changed), Other is Service. | Which consent and frequency rules apply. Marketing needs an opt-in and an unsubscribe link (or opt-out wording in a text), and counts against frequency limits. Transactional, service and legal skip the consent rules, but never a blocked or invalid address. |
| Owner | You, marked Auto-Assigned to You; **Change** opens a searchable list of the workspace's people. | Who is emailed about the campaign's runs and failures (Settings → Notifications), and whose it is in the list. |
| Description (Optional) | Up to 2,000 characters: what the campaign is for. | Shown on the campaign's page. |
| More Options · Code and tags | Folds out the two fields below. Opens by itself when one of them has a problem. | |
| Code (Optional) | Up to 40 characters: capitals, digits and hyphens. Made for you when left blank; fixed once the campaign exists. | The short handle reports, the API and the engine's call log use. A code another campaign uses is refused, naming that campaign. |
| Tags | Up to 20 words; press Enter or a comma after each. The workspace's tags are suggested as you type. | A filter on the Campaigns page. |
| Create Campaign | Saves the draft. | Opens the first step: Audience, or Content for an e-receipt. |

The type and channel can be changed on Edit Details until the content is chosen (a template or a sender; for a drip, a template on any message). After that they are shown locked, with "Fixed Once Content Is Chosen"; to change them, create a new campaign.

### Step 1: Audience

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Send To · Add Contact List | Opens **Add Contact List**: **Existing Lists** (search by name, filter by Type and Source, tick lists or every list shown), **Upload New** (the Contact Lists upload wizard), or **Audience or Clone**. | The lists the campaign goes to. At the run they are merged, one entry per person. A list made from an audience or a clone is attached at once. |
| An uploaded list | Shown as **Needs Review** with **Confirm**, because file data isn't checked upstream. | It counts, and is attached, only once you confirm it. Leaving the step first asks: **Continue Without It** or **Confirm and Continue**. |
| The Send To table | Each list with its source and contact count; the name opens the list in a new tab; **✕** detaches it. The line under it counts the lists and contacts before duplicates and governance. | A list marked Test is flagged: fine for a test publish, refused by a production publish. |
| Don't Send To · Exclude a List | The lists to exclude. A list is included or left out, never both. | Their people are left out whatever other list they are on. |
| Audience | **In Your Lists**, **Unique People** (after duplicates and excluded lists) and **Estimated Recipients** (after today's governance check), with the commonest reason people are left out and a breakdown. | A reading as of today. The real decision is made again when the run starts. |

A draft can be saved without a list; publishing needs at least one.

### Step 2: Content

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Template · Change | The campaign's template: its name and status (and Meta's approval for WhatsApp). **Change** (**Choose a Template** when there is none yet) opens a side panel, **Choose a Template**, with the templates of the campaign's channel and a search box. The picked card shows the message scaled down. **Use Template** applies it; **Cancel**, the cross and Escape leave the template as it was. **Create New Template** opens the template library in a new tab. | The message. A template still in Draft can be tested; a production publish needs it published first. A template of another channel is flagged and must be changed. |
| Subject and Preheader (email and e-receipt templates) | A row that starts closed: **Override** opens it, and it starts open when the campaign already has its own. Subject Line and Preheader, up to 300 characters each; left blank, the template's is used. **Edit the Template's** copies the template's text in to change it; **Use Template's** on the row empties both and closes it. The counter in each field's label row marks where an inbox cuts off (60 characters for a subject, 90 for a preheader), and the smiley button beside it adds an emoji at the cursor; picked while the field is empty, it is added to the template's text, which becomes this campaign's own. Tags such as {{first_name}} work. | Only this campaign's subject and preheader; the template never changes. |
| Topic | The preference-centre topic the campaign belongs to. Asked for marketing campaigns (not e-receipts) when the preference centre is on and has topics for the channel. Needed to publish. | People who unticked the topic on the preference page are left out of this campaign, counted as Topic Opted Out. Topics are managed under Settings → Preference Centre. |
| Sender · Send From (required) | The name and address, or the number, the message comes from; the sender brings its engine. The list starts at **Choose a Sender**. An unverified sender is marked "(not verified)"; one on the workspace's own mail server says so. **New Sender** opens Settings → Sending Engines in a new tab. | Mandatory: **Continue to Test** stays on Content and says *Choose who this campaign comes from* until one is chosen. **Save and Close** still keeps a draft without one. Only a verified sender can publish to production; test sends work from any. A sender added with New Sender is the workspace's and can be used by every campaign. |
| Preview | The chosen template, once the draft is saved. For an email or e-receipt, a desktop / mobile switch; a text shows the phone. **Open in the Designer** (or the Receipt Editor, Message Editor or WhatsApp Editor) opens the template in its own tab. The editor shows **Back to Campaign** at the top and **Save & Return to Campaign**: it saves your changes, closes the editor's tab and brings the campaign back in front, its preview already showing the new version. | Saving in the editor changes the template, so every campaign and journey that uses it. |
| Link Tracking | A row in the same card as Subject and Preheader, shown once a template is chosen. **Edit** (for anyone with Sending Engines · Edit) opens Settings → UTM Tracking in a new tab. | How a campaign's links go out comes from the workspace's settings; the campaign no longer edits them here. The UTM Tracking chapter has the details. |

Both the template and the sender are needed to publish, not to save a draft. A marketing message must let people opt out: an unsubscribe link in an email, opt-out wording such as "Reply STOP" in a text, or the {{opt_out_text}} tag. The pre-publish check looks for it.

### Step 3: Test

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Preview | The message with the catalogue's sample values, or as one customer of the audience would get it; desktop and mobile; click to see it large. | |
| Send a Test | **People of This Workspace** (email only) to tick, and **Other Addresses** or **Mobile Numbers** (with the country code), up to 20, separated by commas; **Send Test**. Needs a template and a sender. | The real message goes out now through the campaign's sender, marked [TEST], with sample values in place of personal data. It is recorded as a test run, counts towards no result, and moves a draft to Test Published. Each address is listed as sent or not sent, with the reason. |
| Subject Line Test (email Regular Marketing and Other campaigns) | **Test a Second Subject Line** switches it on. Off by default. | With it on, each test address gets both subjects, marked [TEST A] and [TEST B]. |
| A | The subject this campaign sends: its own, or the template's. Read-only here; change it on the Content step. | Goes to half of the sample. |
| B | Required, up to 300 characters; tags work. | Goes to the other half of the sample. |
| Sample | 10 to 50 percent of the audience, in steps of 5. Default 20 percent. | The share that gets the test, half A and half B. The rest wait for the winner. With fewer than 20 people in the run there is no split: everyone gets A. |
| Winner Decided By | **Open Rate** (default) or **Click Rate**. | The measure the two subjects are compared on, per message sent. |
| Decide After | 30 minutes, 1, 2, 4 (default), 8 or 12 hours, or 1, 2, 3 or 7 days. | How long after the sample goes out the winner is chosen. If the two are within one point, A wins. The held messages then go with the winning subject. You can also pick the winner yourself on the campaign's Performance tab. |
| Save Subject Test | Saves the test. "Not saved yet." shows while it has changes. | |

### Step 4: Targets (optional, Skip for Now)

| Field | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Opens (not for texts), Clicks, Conversions, Offer Redemptions | What the campaign is meant to achieve. Under each, its share of the estimated recipients. | The Performance tab shows the progress against them. |
| Target Revenue | What the campaign is meant to earn. | Home reads attributed revenue against it, Top Campaigns ranks by revenue against target, and the Performance tab shows the progress. |
| Planned Campaign Cost | Creative, sending and agency costs: $500, $1,000, $2,500, or **Custom** for another amount; **Clear** empties it. | Campaign ROI and the cost line on Home. |
| Planned Offer Cost | Discounts and rewards you expect to give away, chosen the same way. | Total Planned Spend and Planned Return. |
| Notes | Up to 1,000 characters on what the targets need explaining. | Shown on the campaign's page. |
| Plan at a Glance | Projected Cost per Conversion, Target Revenue, Total Planned Spend (both costs) and Planned Return (revenue to spend). | A planning estimate, not a guaranteed outcome. |

**Skip for Now** moves on without targets; they can be filled in later.

### Step 5: Review & Publish

The step has two parts: **When and How It Goes Out**, then **Summary and Publish**. An e-receipt has no schedule: each receipt goes out when the point of sale or the web shop asks for it (with **Change the Campaign Type** if that was not meant).

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| When to Send | **Send Immediately** (the send starts when you publish to production), **Schedule for Later** (one send at a date and time), or **Recurring** (daily, weekly or monthly). | The run or runs the publish creates. A recurring campaign stays one campaign: each occurrence is its own run. |
| Time Zone | The campaign's zone; the workspace's unless changed. The hint names the workspace's zone. | Every time on this step is a clock time in this zone, daylight saving included. The Campaigns list and Home show the send time in this zone too. |
| Send At (Schedule for Later) | The date and time; the hint gives the same moment in the workspace's zone. | When the one send starts. |
| Repeats (Recurring) | **Daily**, **Weekly** or **Monthly**. | How often a run is made. |
| Every (Recurring) | The number of days, weeks or months between runs, 1 to 52. Default 1. | Every 2 weeks, for example. |
| At (Recurring) | The time of day. | When each run starts. |
| On (Weekly) | The days of the week, Mon to Sun. | Which days a weekly campaign runs. |
| Day of the Month (Monthly) | 1 to 31. In a shorter month, the last day is used. | Which day a monthly campaign runs. |
| Ends (Recurring) | **Never (Until Paused or Archived)**, **On a Date** (with **Last Day**), or **After a Number of Sends** (with **Number of Sends**). | When the runs stop. |
| Apply Corporate Frequency Limits | On unless switched off. | Off lifts the workspace's frequency cap for this campaign, and switching it off is recorded in the activity log. Consent, unsubscribes and blocked addresses always apply to marketing; service and transactional messages may be exempt from the cap, but text unsubscribes and blocked addresses always apply. |
| Include Unengaged Contacts | Shown for marketing email. Off unless switched on. | Off: people with no click, purchase or preference visit in the Unengaged Contacts period (Settings → Governance) are left out. On: they get this campaign too, for a win-back campaign; the Summary shows **Unengaged Contacts: Included (a win-back campaign)** and publishing records it in the activity log. Texts and transactional email are never affected. |
| Send at Each Recipient's Local Time | The same clock time in each recipient's own zone, from their address (a US or Canadian state, or a country with one zone) or a time zone attribute. A line says how many people on the attached lists have a known zone. | The send starts as the first zone reaches the time and ends with the last. People without a known zone get the campaign's time. With Send Immediately, people in zones that have not reached the publish time yet get it later today. |
| Messages per Hour, at Most | Optional; blank means no limit of the campaign's own. | Stored and shown on the campaign, not enforced yet. To pace a send, set Messages per Minute on the sending engine under Settings → Sending Engines. |
| Quiet hours (note) | Shown for marketing texts. | Marketing texts are held during the workspace's quiet hours and start when they end. |
| The summary card | Names anything unfinished (the checklist's problems, no test sent, no targets), each with the way to it. | |
| Summary | Everything chosen, part by part, with an **Edit** link to each. | |
| Before You Publish | The pre-publish checklist: **Template content finalized** (a published template with content; in marketing, the unsubscribe link in an email or opt-out wording in a text; a picture in an MMS), **Campaign List attached** (a Production list), **Verified sender chosen**, and, when they need attention, **Topic chosen**, **Send time set** and **Recurrence valid**; **Approved for production** when approvals are required. **Test message sent and reviewed** is advice only. | **Publish to Production** stays greyed out until every required item is done. A missing test only warns. |
| Audience Estimate | The estimate as of today. | |
| Approval | Shown when the workspace requires approval (Settings → Approvals). **Note to the Approver**: optional, up to 500 characters, what to check or why it is ready; **Request Approval** (or **Request Approval Again** after a send-back) emails everyone who can approve. An approver sees a **Note**, which goes to the person who asked and is required to **Send Back**, and **Approve**. | Publish to Production waits until it is approved. Nobody approves their own campaign. |
| Publish to Production | Needs `campaign.publish`. Asks once more in **Publish to production?**: who it goes to, when, and a warning when no test was sent; **Not Yet** or **Publish**. | Creates the runs. After publishing the campaign can no longer be edited; it can be paused, and a scheduled send can be cancelled. |

## How to

**Set up a one-time email.** Create Campaign → name it, Regular Marketing, Email (Message Purpose becomes Marketing) → Create Campaign → Audience: Add Contact List, tick the list → Content: pick the template, check the subject line, choose the topic and the sender → Test: send yourself a test → Targets: Skip for Now or enter the revenue target → Review & Publish: Send Immediately or Schedule for Later → Publish to Production.

**Change the template while setting up.** On the Content step, **Open in the Designer** (or the editor for the channel) under the preview. Edit in the new tab, then **Save & Return to Campaign**: the editor saves, its tab closes, and the Content step shows the new version. Carry on with Test, Targets and Review & Publish. A template used by a live drip cannot be changed: the button then reads **Return to Campaign** and only takes you back.

**Rehearse without sending to anyone.** Attach a Test list and send tests to colleagues from the Test step. A test send moves the status to Test Published and reaches nobody else.

**Send a series.** Drip on Create Campaign → Audience: tick the list → Drip Campaign Setup: build message 1, then Add Message (a copy of the last keeps the header and footer), give each its day and subject, choose the start date, the send time and the zone, and the sender → Targets → Test & Publish: Send All as a Test, check each message, then Publish to Production.

**Send every Monday at 9:00.** Review & Publish → Recurring → Repeats: Weekly, Every 1, At 09:00, On: Mon → Ends: Never (Until Paused or Archived), or After a Number of Sends.

**Send at everyone's 10 a.m.** Tick Send at Each Recipient's Local Time and set Send At to 10:00. The step says how many people have a known zone; the others get 10:00 in the campaign's zone.

**Test two subject lines.** On the Test step switch on Test a Second Subject Line, write B, keep the sample at 20 percent and the decision at four hours, and press Save Subject Test. The Performance tab shows A and B side by side once the run starts.

## A Drip Campaign

A drip sends several messages on a timetable to one audience on one channel. Everyone on the campaign list starts together on the start date; each message goes on its day (Day 0 is the start date) at the series time, or at its own time.

| Step | What you do there |
|---|---|
| Audience | As for any campaign. Everyone eligible starts the drip together on its start date: people added to the lists after message 1 don't join, and consent and frequency limits are checked again before each message. |
| Drip Campaign Setup | The messages as a timeline of cards, in day order, with the gap between them. Open a card to set its name (it names the template copy too), its day, its own send time, the template (Choose from Gallery, Edit Template, Change), the subject line and preheader (email) or the text (SMS, MMS), and the A/B subject test (one message per drip, email only). Add Message starts from a copy of the last message, a template from the gallery, or a blank one; Copy as Next Message does the same from the open one; Remove Message asks first. Series at a Glance holds the start date, the send time and the time zone, what the series adds up to and a calendar; Sender (and Topic) are set once for every message. Changing the date, time or zone moves every message's date but never changes the messages. Continue lists the messages that still need something, with Show First Problem. |
| Targets | As for any campaign; the targets are for the drip as a whole. |
| Test & Publish | Preview each message (as a sample customer, on desktop or mobile). Send All as a Test sends every message now, one after another, to the people you pick; each subject starts with its place: "[Test 2 of 3 · would send Day 3]" for email, "[Test 2/3 · Day 3]" for texts, and the A/B message sends both subjects. A message edited after its test says Changed since the test; Resend it. The Review Timeline shows each message's date, subject and reach, with warnings for messages closer than two days, frequency limits and quiet hours. Before You Publish lists what's left; Publish to Production plans every message at once. |

When an Unengaged Contacts rule is in use, Test & Publish says so under the timeline: unengaged contacts are left out of the drip's emails ("people with no engagement in 180 days, after at least 5 marketing emails"), with the share of the workspace's email contacts that is today, or, on a new install, the day the rule starts.
