A drip has no Include Unengaged Contacts switch.

Each message has its own copy of its template ("Loyalty Welcome Series · 2 of 4"), so editing one never changes another. From the editor, Back to Drip Campaign Setup returns to the message. A message's own subject line is what it sends; its template copy needs none.

Once published, the campaign page shows the Series Timeline: each message Sent with its figures, Sending, Next Up, Scheduled, On Hold, Cancelled or Failed, and who was skipped and why (View People lists them). Pause holds the waiting messages; Resume first shows when the missed ones will go. Stop Remaining Messages cancels the rest for good (to send again, clone it). A failed message nobody received can be retried. Clone asks for the copy's name and start date and opens its Drip Campaign Setup step.

**Not in the first version:** people can't join a drip after it starts (no rolling entry), conversions and revenue aren't split by message, there's no view of where one person is in the series, test recipients can't be saved as a list, and a change can't be applied to every message at once.

## What happens behind the scenes

1. Continue validates the step and saves the draft; the API's field messages land on the same fields and the step that holds the first one opens. A save against an older version is refused and the page offers to reload the latest version.
2. The set-up remembers how far it got: an unfinished draft opens at the next step and the steps ahead stay closed, so the publish cannot be reached early.
3. The audience estimate runs the send engine's own governance order as of today; the frozen list at the run is the record of what a send used.
4. Production Publish runs the checklist, records who published, waits for the approval when the workspace requires one, and creates the runs the dispatcher will start on time.

## Tag Check

On the Audience step and again on Review & Publish, the **Tag Check** card takes the template's tags one by one and says, for the people of the chosen lists (after the lists left out), how many have a value and where it comes from: a list column, a customer attribute, or the customer record, in the order the send looks. Its tag reads *2 Tags Short* or *All Tags Have Values*; when every tag has a value the card is one line, with **Details**. Under the table it says what the others get: the tag's default, or nothing. Before the template is chosen it says to choose it on the Content step.

**Fill From Database** (with `list.edit`) reads the short tags from the Report Database behind a customer attribute (choose the field for each tag) for these people, by their ID, and stores the values with their list entries where the entry has none. It runs in the background; the card counts again when it ends. Lists used by other campaigns get the values too. An upload made from the campaign asks the same question on its **Tag Values** step (Contact Lists).

## Estimated Cost

For an SMS, MMS or WhatsApp campaign, people with View Message Costs see an **Estimated Cost** card beside the audience on the Audience step, and again on Review & Publish (Settings → Message Costs, once the platform team has set prices). Only people who can receive the campaign are counted. Once the template is picked, each person's own text is filled in and its parts counted, so a long name or an emoji shows up as extra parts; before that the figure is a *From* price, one part each. Above 50,000 people a sample is counted, and the card says so. A WhatsApp estimate is by country and assumes every message is delivered.

The card's tag says whether the estimate is within the workspace's limits or over its approval limit or budget. Over the approval limit (or over the budget, when the workspace asks for approval then) the campaign needs an approver: its Approval card appears on Review & Publish, as when Settings → Approvals is on. Over a budget set to Block Publishing, Publish to Production is refused. The *Publish to production?* confirmation shows the estimate and the month's spend after it. On Targets & Economics, **Use the Estimate** puts the figure in Planned Campaign Cost.

## Things to know

- The type and channel are fixed once the content (a template or a sender) is chosen; until then Edit Details can change them. After that, make a new campaign.
- A Test list blocks a production publish; an unverified sender blocks it too, while test sends still work.
- A campaign's own subject line never changes the template; other campaigns on the same template keep theirs.
- The hourly limit is not enforced yet; pace a send with the engine's Messages per Minute.
- A drip's messages can't change once it is published, and their templates are locked (read-only, not deletable) until it finishes or is stopped.

---

# Templates and Content

**Where:** Templates in the main navigation; a template opens in its own editor: the email designer, the message editor for texts, or the receipt editor. A campaign's **Open in the …** opens the same editors in their own tab, with a way back to the campaign (see *Opened from a campaign* below).

## What this page is for

A template is the content a campaign sends: an email design, a text message, or an e-receipt. The Templates page is the library: start a new one from a channel or from a C360 preset, and find, rename, duplicate or preview the ones you have. Each channel has its own editor, full-screen, with versions kept on every save. A campaign picks a template on its Content step; a template is used by any number of campaigns and changing it changes what those campaigns send from then on.

## Who can use it

| | |
|---|---|
| Permission | `template.edit` to create, edit and activate; without it the library is read-only and previews still work. |
| Features | SMS and MMS templates need **sms** and **mms**; E-Receipt needs **ereceipt**; the Barcode and QR Code blocks need **dynamic_codes**. |

## The fields and controls

### The Templates page

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Start a new message | One card per channel you may use: Email, E-Receipt, SMS, MMS. | Opens New Template for that channel. |
| Your Templates | Search, a tab per channel, a Status filter (Draft, Active, Archived). | The library below. |
| A template's card | Name, channel, status, when it was last used and by which campaign. A drip message's template says which message it is ("Loyalty Welcome Series · Message 2 of 4"); a drip's templates sit together by name, and carry Locked while the drip is live. | Opens the editor. |
| Card menu | Preview, Rename, Duplicate (a draft copy of the latest version), Delete. | Delete is refused while a campaign uses the template, and while a drip uses it for a message ("This Template Is in Use", with the way to the campaign). |
| Starting Point gallery | C360's presets by category. A preset opens in a read-only preview first. | Copy This Template makes it yours as a new draft; the preset itself never changes. |

### New Template

| Field | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Name | Up to 200 characters. | How it is listed, and what a campaign's Content step shows. |
| Channel | Email (the drag-and-drop designer), E-Receipt (the receipt editor), SMS (the message editor with part counting), MMS (text plus one picture). Fixed once created. | Which editor opens and which campaigns can use it. |
| Description | What the template is for. | Shown in the library. |
| Starting Point | Blank, or one of the starters. | Version 1 of the design. |

A new template is a draft. Activate it from its editor when it is ready; only an active template can be published in a campaign.

### The email designer

The short tour; the chapter The Email Designer has every block and setting, and worked examples.

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Subject, Preheader | The template's subject line and the text an inbox shows after it. | The campaign uses them unless it writes its own. A missing subject blocks Activate. |
| The palette | Structure (one to four columns), Content (Heading, Text, Image, Button, Divider, Spacer, Social, Header, Footer, Custom HTML), Commerce (Product Card, Coupon, Barcode and QR Code where the feature is on). Drag a block onto the canvas or focus a tile and press Enter. | The design. |
| The canvas | The email as it will render, with desktop and phone views; a block selected shows its settings in the panel. | |
| The settings panel | Per block: text, links, images, colours, fonts, alignment; Margin and Padding; Border and Shadow; Hide on Phones and Hide on Desktop; a barcode or QR code's source tag, format and size. | Written into the email's MJML, the format the server renders to HTML. |
| Tags | The workspace's personalization tags, inserted at the cursor as {{first_name}} and the like. Unsubscribe URL, current year, today, campaign name and email are always available. | Filled in per recipient at send time. |
| Links | Every link in the design, checked for an address. | A missing or bad address is listed before Activate. |
| Errors | Findings: unknown tags, a missing subject, no unsubscribe link, HTML over Gmail's 102 KB clip limit. | Warnings on Save; blockers on Activate. A marketing campaign cannot publish without the unsubscribe link. |
| Preview, Send a Test | The compiled email with sample values, desktop and mobile; a test to your own address. | |
| Save, Activate | Save writes version n+1 and never overwrites an earlier one; Activate makes the template usable by campaigns. | A save on top of someone else's newer version is refused and the page offers to reload. |
| History | Every version with who saved it and when; open one and save to make it the newest. | |
| Code | The MJML source, for reading or pasting a block of raw markup. | |

### The message editor (SMS, MMS)

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Subject (MMS) | Optional, up to 80 characters. | Sent with the MMS; some phones show it. |
| Message | The text, up to 1,600 characters (10 parts). Under it, live counting: characters, parts and characters per part, what is left in this part, and the alphabet (GSM-7 or UCS-2). A text over 160 GSM characters, or over 70 once it holds an emoji or a character outside the standard SMS alphabet, is sent as more than one part, and the carrier bills each. With tags, the count uses their sample values; with UTM tracking on for texts, links to your sites are counted as short links. | What is sent. A longer text cannot be saved. |
| Insert Tag | The workspace's personalization tags, by group, put in at the cursor. | Filled in per recipient at send time. |
| Emoji | The emoji picker, put in at the cursor. | Makes the text a Unicode (UCS-2) message: 70 characters a part. |
| Use Plain Punctuation | Shown when curly quotes, long dashes, ellipses or invisible spaces push the text out of the standard SMS alphabet. Swaps them for plain ones. Also in a drip's text messages, with the characters named. | Brings the text back to 160 characters a part. |
| Pasting | Text pasted from Word, Google Docs or a web page: curly quotes (’ “ ”), long dashes (– —), ellipses (…) and invisible spaces become plain as they land. A note says how many changed; **Keep as Pasted** puts them back. | Keeps one stray character from turning the whole message into Unicode (70 characters a part, 67 when split, instead of 160 and 153). |
| At send time | Nothing to set. Just before a text goes to the gateway, C360 makes the same swap in the finished message, after the tags are filled, so a curly apostrophe in someone's own data (O’Brien, a store name) does not double their parts. A message that is Unicode anyway (an emoji, a non-Latin script) keeps its punctuation as written. | The Recipients tab shows the text as it was sent. Emoji are never changed: they keep a message in Unicode. |
| Picture (MMS) | **Choose from Library** (the workspace's images) or **Upload**: JPEG, PNG or GIF, up to 1 MB; under 600 KB is delivered reliably by every carrier. **Remove** takes it off. | The picture the MMS carries. An MMS without one cannot be published in a campaign. |
| Before Sending | Warnings as you type: characters outside the standard alphabet, emoji, more than three parts, unknown tags (sent as typed), and no opt-out wording. | Advice; the text can still be saved. |
| Opt-out wording | A marketing text must say how to stop, with words such as STOP, unsubscribe or opt out, or the **Opt-Out Text** tag {{opt_out_text}} ("Reply STOP to opt out. HELP for help. Msg&data rates may apply."). The tag's words are kept in one place, the platform's tag catalogue in the back-office, so every text says the same and a change there reaches every message. Without opt-out wording, **Before Sending** offers **Add the Opt-Out Text**, which adds the tag at the end. | A marketing campaign on a text without it fails the pre-publish check and cannot be published. |
| The preview | The message in a phone frame, from the workspace's default texting sender, with the tags' sample values. | |
| History, Activate, Save | **History** lists every saved version; open one and Save to make it the newest. **Activate** (on a draft, once saved) makes the template usable by campaigns. **Save** writes a new version. | As in the designer. The editor is read-only for someone without `template.edit`, for an archived template, and while a live drip uses it. |

### The receipt editor

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Layout | **This Template’s Own Layout**, or a layout from Templates → Receipt Layouts. With a library layout picked the settings below are read-only: **Edit Layout** opens it, **Make a Copy for This Template** turns it back into this template’s own. **Save to Receipt Layouts** puts this template’s own layout into the library. | Where the receipt comes from. A library layout reaches every template that picks it. |
| Presentation | Compact, Standard, Itemized, Store Register, or Custom HTML. | The shape of the receipt inside the email. |
| Receipt Sections | Order header, Customer & fulfillment, Items, Totals, Payment, Loyalty, Returns & support: each on or off (Order header, Items and Totals are always shown). Select a section to see its own settings, below. | What the point of sale's transaction is laid out as, at send time. |

The sections' settings:

| Section | Setting | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Order header | Order Number, Order Date, Store or Channel, Receipt ID | A switch each. Receipt ID is needed for expense and tax claims. | Which of these lines the receipt's header shows. |
| Customer & fulfillment | Address Shown | **Auto from Order** (the delivery address when the order has one, else the pickup place or store), **Ship To**, or **Pickup At**. | Whether the section reads Delivery with the shipping address, or In store with the pickup place or store. An order without that address says "No address on this order". |
| | Delivery Estimate | On or off. | On: the delivery or pickup estimate the transaction carries ("Arrives Thu, Sep 25", "Ready for pickup Sep 21, 4:00 PM") is shown under the address. A transaction without one shows nothing, whatever the switch says. |
| Items | Product Image, SKU, Quantity, Unit Price, Amount; Row Height (Compact or Default) | The item table's columns and how tall its rows are. | |
| Totals | Subtotal, Discounts, Tax, Shipping, Tip; Savings Callout | The totals rows, and a highlighted line on the discount under the total. | Discounts, Shipping and Tip show only when the transaction has them, and Shipping never on an in-store (POS) receipt. The Savings Callout shows only when the transaction has a discount. |
| Payment | Payment Method, Authorization Time, Billing Address | Payment Method is always masked to the last four digits. | |
| Loyalty | Points Earned, Points Balance, Tier Progress | A switch each. | Points Balance on: the customer's points balance after this purchase, as the transaction reports it, shown as "Points balance". The whole section shows only when the transaction says the customer is in the loyalty programme; Tier Progress only when the transaction carries it ("620 points to Gold"). |
| Returns & support | Policy Text, In-Store Return Barcode | The returns wording, and a barcode the store can scan for a return. | |
| Show Inside Email Shell | Whether the receipt sits inside the outer email design. | |
| The outer design | The email around the receipt: brand header, marketing block, button, image, divider, footer, in the same designer. | |
| Sample Transaction | A POS or eCommerce sample to preview with; desktop and mobile; Send a Test. | |
| Checks | What a receipt needs before Activate. | |

The Store Register's settings (select a part under Receipt Parts):

| Part | Setting | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Title & Header | The title (YOUR RECEIPT), and the six transaction fields: rename, hide, reorder. | The block at the top, in rows of three. |
| Columns | One line per column: its header, and what it shows when the two differ (Qty, Shows Quantity). The pencil (**Edit**) opens the column's Header and Shows; **Done** closes it. Add up to 5 columns (Size, Colour, Quantity, Unit Price, Discount, Line Tax, SKU, UPC, Status); a new one opens for editing. Remove or reorder them. The item is always first and its amount always last. Under the item: Discount, Qty @ Price, SKU, UPC. | The item table. Every value comes from the point of sale's transaction. |
| Totals & Tenders | Rename or hide Subtotal, the taxes, Paid with and Cash Change (TOTAL is always shown). Taxes: **With the rate**. Paid with: **With the authorisation**; an empty label prints the tender alone (CREDIT-VISA). TOTAL: **A size larger**. **Add Row**: Order Discount (with its code), You Saved (the lines' discounts and the order's, in the discount colour), Shipping, Tip, Items Sold, Points Earned, Points Balance. **Layout**: **Beside the Figures** (Subtotal: right beside its figure) or **Label Left** (no colons). | The rows under the items, always in the same order. The figures come from the transaction; a row with nothing for a receipt (no shipping, no loyalty) drops out of that receipt. |
| Colours & Text | **Theme**: Lilac (as before), Paper, Ink or Blue. **Customise Colours**: Frame, Cards, Column Headers, Text, Discounts and Savings, by picker or hex code. **Around the Receipt**: Choose a Colour fills the space between the frame and the email's edges; Use the Email's Background goes back to the email's own (as before). **Font**: Helvetica, Georgia or Courier. **Text Size**: Small, Medium, Large. **Title**: size and Centre or Left. **Labels**: bold header labels; column headers in capitals. | The whole receipt. Text on a dark colour turns white by itself, so it stays readable. Only fonts every mail app has are offered. |
| Footer Text | Up to 500 characters with tags, centred or left. | Text inside the receipt under the totals; a tag that is empty for a receipt drops out. |
| On Phones | **Fold Extra Columns Under the Item** or **Keep Every Column**. | On a phone, folded columns move to a line under the item. Mail apps that ignore phone rules show every column. |

A Custom HTML receipt has three parts: **Receipt** (the HTML around the lines, with `{{receipt_items}}` where the rows go), **Item Row** (one line, repeated per item, with `{{item.description}}`, `{{item.sku}}`, `{{item.upc}}`, `{{item.size}}`, `{{item.color}}`, `{{item.quantity}}`, `{{item.unit_price}}`, `{{item.discount}}`, `{{item.tax}}`, `{{item.amount}}`, `{{item.status}}`) and **CSS**. Without an Item Row, `{{receipt_body}}` still gives the built-in item table.

### Opened from a campaign

Every editor (email designer, receipt editor, message editor for SMS and MMS, WhatsApp editor) opened from a campaign's Content step or Content tab works the same way:

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Back to Campaign | Top left, in place of the link back to Templates. | Goes back to the campaign in this tab; unsaved changes ask first. |
| Save & Return to Campaign | Bottom right in the email designer and the receipt editor; beside Save in the message editor and the WhatsApp editor. *Return to Campaign* when the template cannot be changed (a live drip uses it). | Saves your changes (when there are any; a text or a WhatsApp message must be valid first), then closes the editor's tab so the campaign is in front again. If the browser does not let the tab close, the campaign opens in this tab. A refused save keeps you here and says why. |

Every save in an editor tells the campaign's tab, so its preview shows the new version without a reload.

### Receipt Layouts

Templates → **Receipt Layouts** lists the receipt layouts E-Receipt templates can pick, with how many templates use each. **New Layout** starts one from Store Register, Standard, Itemized, Compact, Custom HTML, or a copy of another; each row's menu has Edit, Duplicate, Rename and Archive. Editing a layout used by templates names them before you save, because the change reaches all of them; a layout used by a template in a running drip cannot be changed until the drip ends. A layout a template still uses cannot be archived.

## How to

**Make an email from a preset.** Templates → scroll to Starting Point → open a preset → Copy This Template → name it → the designer opens with the copy; change the text and images, Save, Activate.

**Personalize.** Put the cursor in a text block, open Tags, pick First Name. The canvas shows the sample value; each recipient gets their own.

**Add a coupon with a barcode.** Commerce → Barcode; in the settings pick the source tag (for example the coupon code uploaded with the list) and the format. The sent email carries each recipient's own code; a recipient without a value gets the email without the block.

**Hide a block on phones.** Select it → Hide on Phones. The phone view of the canvas shows the result.

**Go back to an earlier version.** History → open the version → Save. It becomes the newest version; nothing is lost.

**Write a text that fits in one part.** Keep under 160 characters without emoji; the counter says how many parts the text will be sent as.

## What happens behind the scenes

1. The designer saves the design twice over: the editor's own state, to reopen exactly as left, and the MJML source. The server renders the HTML from the MJML, derives the text part, and checks the design (tags, subject, unsubscribe link, size). The browser never supplies the final HTML.
2. Every save is a new version in the template's history; Activate marks the template usable by campaigns.
3. At send time the engine fills the tags from the workspace's tag catalogue, the recipient's list columns and the customer record, draws each barcode and QR code, puts the campaign's subject and preheader in when it has its own, and for a receipt lays out the transaction the point of sale sent into the receipt slot.
4. Images are uploaded once per workspace and served from the media store, never embedded in the email.

## The sender on a text preview

The phone in a text template's editor and previews shows the sender at the top: the workspace's default SMS sender; for an MMS template its MMS sender first, then the SMS one. Opened from a campaign (Edit Template), it shows that campaign's own sender. A workspace with no text sender reads *No SMS Sender* (or *No MMS Sender*), and the editor adds an **Add a Sender** link that opens Settings → Sending Engines in a new tab, so the template you are working on stays open.

## What a text costs

With View Message Costs, and once the platform team has set prices (Settings → Message Costs), a line under an SMS or MMS template's part counter says what one recipient costs at the default sender's price, and per 1,000: parts × (price a part + carrier fee) for SMS, one flat price for MMS. It updates as you type: an emoji makes every part shorter (70 characters instead of 160), so the line shows the extra parts. Tags are counted with their sample values, so a long value can add a part. A WhatsApp template shows the price of its category in the home country instead: length and pictures do not change it.

## Things to know

- Changing a template changes what every campaign using it sends from then on. Duplicate when a campaign must keep its own version.
- A drip gives every message its own copy of a template, so a drip's messages never change other campaigns. Opened from the Drip Campaign Setup step the editor says which message it is, keeps the subject on the message (read-only here), and leads Back to Drip Campaign Setup. While the drip is live the copy is locked: the preview and the editor say "Used by a live drip (…). It can't be changed until the drip finishes or is stopped." and open read-only.
- Only an active template can be published; a draft can be previewed and test sent.
- A marketing email must carry the unsubscribe link; the designer blocks Activate without it and the campaign cannot publish.
- Outlook for Windows ignores shadows; the field says so.
- An SMS template cannot have a subject line, and a campaign on it cannot either; an MMS may carry the optional Subject above.

---

# WhatsApp Templates

**Where:** Templates → the WhatsApp tab; a WhatsApp template opens in the WhatsApp editor. The number itself is connected under Settings → Delivery. Your administrator has the same steps with screenshots in the Word guide *CEP WhatsApp Set-Up Guide*.

## What this page is for

WhatsApp lets a business start a conversation only with a template Meta has approved. A WhatsApp template is written here with the workspace's tags, sent to Meta for review with **Submit to Meta**, and sent by campaigns once Meta approves it. Templates made directly in Meta's WhatsApp Manager appear here too, through **Sync from Meta**. Every workspace uses its own Meta business account: CEP never sends through a shared one.

## Who can use it

| | |
|---|---|
| Permissions | `provider.edit` to connect the number (Settings → Delivery); `template.edit` to write, submit, sync and activate templates; the usual campaign permissions to send. |
| Feature | **whatsapp** (WhatsApp messages), switched on for the workspace by the platform team. Off by default. |
| Outside CEP | A Meta business portfolio, a Meta app with the WhatsApp product, a WhatsApp business account with a phone number, a payment method on that account, and a system-user access token. Section *Set it up from start to finish* lists each one. |

## The fields and controls

### The WhatsApp editor

Opened from a campaign's Content, it shows **Back to Campaign** and **Save & Return to Campaign** beside Save (Templates and Content: *Opened from a campaign*).

| Field | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| WhatsApp Number | The connected number (engine) the template is for. | The Meta business account that reviews and sends it. |
| Name at Meta | Lower-case letters, digits and underscores, for example `back_in_stock`. | How Meta knows the template. Fixed once Meta has it. |
| Category | Marketing, or Utility for an update the person expects (a receipt, an order). | Meta charges by category, and moves anything promotional to Marketing. |
| Language | The language the words are in. | A template has one language; another language is another template. |
| Header | None, Text (up to 60 characters, the same for everyone), a Picture (JPEG or PNG up to 5 MB) or a Document (PDF). | Shown above the message. Meta reviews a picture or document header with the file you give it, and sends that file with every message. |
| Body | The message, up to 1,024 characters, with tags such as `{{first_name}}`. | What people read. Meta gets it with numbered variables (`{{1}}`, `{{2}}`) in the order the tags first appear. |
| Samples for Meta's Review | A sample value for every tag in the body. | Meta reviews the message with them. |
| If Empty | What to send when a person has no value for a tag. | Meta refuses a message with an empty value: without a fallback (or the tag's own default), that person's message fails. |
| Footer | A small grey line, up to 60 characters, for example *Reply STOP to opt out*. | Shown under the message. |
| Buttons | Up to 10: Link (at most 2), Quick Reply, Phone Number (at most 1). | A quick reply that starts with *Stop* opts the person out of WhatsApp when tapped. |
| Save | A new version, still a draft. | Nothing goes to Meta yet. |
| Submit to Meta | Sends the saved version for review. | The template is In Review; the status updates by itself when Meta decides, usually within minutes, at most 24 hours. |
| Copy to Change | A draft copy, under a name of its own. | A template in review or approved does not change: Meta sends exactly what it approved. The copy is reviewed again. |
| Activate | Makes an approved template usable by campaigns. | A template written here becomes active by itself when Meta approves it. |

### Where Meta's review stands

| Status | What it means |
|---|---|
| Not Submitted | Written here, not yet with Meta. |
| In Review | Meta is reviewing it. |
| Approved | Campaigns can send it. |
| Rejected | Meta's reason is shown; change it and submit it again. |
| Paused by Meta | Meta paused it for low quality (many people blocked or reported it). Campaigns cannot send it until Meta lifts the pause. |
| Disabled by Meta | Meta switched it off; copy it to make a new version. |

### A template made in WhatsApp Manager

Sync from Meta brings in every template of the number's business account. One made in WhatsApp Manager keeps Meta's words: the editor shows them with Meta's numbers and asks which tag fills each number, and what to send when it is empty. It can be used once every number has a tag.

## Set it up from start to finish

The example throughout is a retailer, *City HT*, in Singapore, with the WhatsApp number +65 6812 4400. Every ID and value below is a made-up example: use your own.

### Step 1: at Meta (the workspace's own accounts)

1. **Business portfolio.** At business.facebook.com, create or choose the business portfolio (for example *City HT Pte Ltd*) and complete Meta's business verification (Settings → Security Centre). Unverified businesses can message far fewer people a day.
2. **App.** At developers.facebook.com → My Apps → Create App, create a *Business* app (for example *City HT Messaging*) connected to that portfolio, and add the **WhatsApp** product to it. Meta creates a WhatsApp business account and a test number with it.
3. **Phone number.** In WhatsApp Manager → Phone Numbers, add the real number (+65 6812 4400), verify it by SMS or voice call, and set the display name (*City HT*). Meta approves the display name. The number must not be in use on the WhatsApp or WhatsApp Business app.
4. **Payment method.** In WhatsApp Manager → Billing (or Business Settings → Payments), add the card or credit line Meta charges per delivered message.
5. **System user and token.** In Business Settings → Users → System Users, add a system user (role Admin), assign it the app and the WhatsApp account with full control, then **Generate New Token**: choose the app, expiry *Never*, and the permissions `whatsapp_business_messaging` and `whatsapp_business_management`. Copy the token: Meta shows it once.
6. **Collect five values.** WhatsApp Business Account ID (for example `104883925517204`) and Phone Number ID (for example `118204576330981`) from WhatsApp Manager or the app's WhatsApp → API Setup page; the App ID and App Secret from the app's App Settings → Basic; and the token from step 5.

> The temporary token on the API Setup page lasts 24 hours: never use it in CEP.

### Step 2: in the back office (platform team)

1. Back office → Tenants → open the workspace (for example *City HT*).
2. Under **Industry and Features**, set **WhatsApp messages** to *On*, and save.
3. Within a minute the workspace offers the WhatsApp engine, the WhatsApp tab in Templates and the WhatsApp channel in campaigns.

### Step 3: connect the number (Settings → Delivery)

1. **Add Engine** → Engine *WhatsApp · WhatsApp (Meta)*. Name: *City HT WhatsApp*. WhatsApp Business Account ID, Phone Number ID, Access Token and App Secret from step 1; App ID if templates will have a picture or document header. Leave Graph API Version empty. **Add Engine**.
2. In the engine's row, **Copy Webhook URL** and **Copy Verify Token**.
3. In the Meta app: WhatsApp → Configuration → Webhook → **Edit**. Paste the callback URL (for example `https://cepdev.aiintelekt.com/api/v1/webhooks/delivery/CITYHT/7f3c…/a1b2…/`) and the verify token, and **Verify and Save**. Meta calls the address once and CEP answers; a refusal here means the token or the address was not copied whole.
4. Under Webhook fields, **Subscribe** to `messages` and `message_template_status_update` (and, if you want them, `message_template_quality_update` and `phone_number_quality_update`).
5. Back in CEP, press **Check** on the engine. The row then reads, for example, *+65 6812 4400 · City HT · Quality High · 1,000 people a day*.
6. **Add Sender**: Sends Through *City HT WhatsApp*, Name *City HT WhatsApp*, WhatsApp Number *+65 6812 4400*. Then **Verify**: CEP asks Meta whether the engine's number is this one, and the sender shows *Verified*.

### Step 4: record WhatsApp consent

WhatsApp is sent only to people with a WhatsApp opt-in, for every kind of campaign; consent to texts does not count. A list upload records it from a *WhatsApp consent date* column, for example:

| email | first_name | mobile | whatsapp_consent_date |
|---|---|---|---|
| mei.ling@example.com | Mei Ling | +6591234471 | 2026-10-01 |
| arif.rahman@example.com | Arif | +60124481902 | 2026-10-03 |

Numbers need their country code. A date in the future, or one not recognised, records no consent.

### Step 5: write a template and have Meta approve it

1. Templates → **WhatsApp** card (or the WhatsApp tab → New Template). Name it *Weekend Offer*; start from **WhatsApp offer** or from blank.
2. In the editor: WhatsApp Number *+6568124400 · City HT*; Name at Meta `weekend_offer`; Category *Marketing*; Language *English*; Header *None*.
3. Body, for example: *Hi {{first_name}}, this weekend only: 20% off everything in store. Show this message at the till to get it.*
4. Samples: `first_name` → *Mei Ling*. If Empty: `first_name` → *there*.
5. Footer *Reply STOP to opt out*; Buttons: Quick Reply *Stop Offers*.
6. **Save**, then **Submit to Meta**. The status is *In Review*; when Meta decides, it becomes *Approved* (and the template Active) or *Rejected* with Meta's reason.

### Step 6: send a campaign

1. Campaigns → Create Campaign: type *Regular Marketing*, channel **WhatsApp**.
2. Content: choose *Weekend Offer* (it shows *Approved*); Sender *City HT WhatsApp*.
3. Audience: the list from step 4.
4. **Test Publish** to your own WhatsApp number: the approved template arrives with the samples Meta reviewed.
5. **Publish**. Meta's reports fill the campaign's Recipients tab (Delivered, Read, Failed with Meta's reason) and its results (Read beside Delivered).

## Test it

Run these in order on a new set-up; each says what to look for.

| # | Test | Do | Expect |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The connection | Settings → Delivery → **Check** on the engine. | The number, name, quality and daily limit appear in the engine's row. A refusal names Meta's reason (most often the token). |
| 2 | The callback address | Meta's **Verify and Save** in step 3. | Meta accepts it. In CEP the engine's Callbacks column shows a callback once Meta sends its first event. |
| 3 | Sync | Templates → WhatsApp → **Sync from Meta**. | Meta's sample template `hello_world` (on a new account) and any template made in WhatsApp Manager appear, with Meta's status. |
| 4 | A review round trip | Write a short Utility template and **Submit to Meta**. | *In Review*, then *Approved* within minutes, with no action in CEP: Meta's callback arrives. If it stays *In Review* for an hour, test 2 is not done. |
| 5 | A refusal before Meta | Make the body end with a tag, for example *Your store is {{preferred_store}}*. | The editor says Meta would refuse it, and Save stays off. |
| 6 | A test send | A WhatsApp campaign → **Test Publish** to your own number. | The message arrives on your phone. |
| 7 | Consent | Publish to a small list: one person with WhatsApp consent, one with text consent only. | The first gets it; the second is left out as *No Consent*. |
| 8 | Read | Open the message on the phone. | The campaign's Recipients tab shows the time it was read. |
| 9 | Stop | Tap **Stop Offers** in the message. | The person is opted out of WhatsApp (Recipients: *Opted Out*); their text consent is unchanged. |
| 10 | A number that cannot receive | Add a person whose number is not on WhatsApp. | *Failed: Not a WhatsApp number, or it cannot receive this message (Meta 131026)*. |

## What happens behind the scenes

- A WhatsApp message goes to people with a WhatsApp opt-in only, for every kind of campaign: Meta's rule. Consent to texts is not consent to WhatsApp.
- A text STOP does not stop WhatsApp, and a WhatsApp STOP (typed, or a Stop quick reply) stops WhatsApp only.
- Meta reports Sent, Delivered, Read and Failed for each message; the campaign's Recipients tab shows Read, and its results count reads.
- Each person's values fill the approved template; the record of what they got is the template's words with their values.
- Callbacks are checked with the App Secret (Meta's signature) as well as the address's own token, and the same callback delivered twice is applied once.

## When something goes wrong

| What you see | Why | What to do |
|---|---|---|
| Check: *Meta refused the access token* (Meta 190) | The token expired, was revoked, or is the 24-hour one. | Generate a system-user token with expiry Never (step 1.5) and paste it under Edit. |
| Meta's Verify and Save fails | The callback URL or verify token was not copied whole, or WhatsApp is not on for the workspace. | Copy both again from the engine's row. |
| Templates stay *In Review*; nothing on Recipients | Meta is not calling back: webhook fields not subscribed, or the app is not subscribed to the WhatsApp account. | Subscribe `messages` and `message_template_status_update`; if the account was not created by the app, subscribe the app to the account in Meta (subscribed apps). |
| Meta says it sends callbacks, but the engine's Callbacks column never shows one | CEP refuses them: the App Secret in CEP is not this app's, so Meta's signature does not match. | Paste the right App Secret under Edit, or leave it empty to rely on the address's own token. |
| *Failed … (Meta 131026)* | The number is not on WhatsApp, or cannot receive it. | Nothing to fix in CEP; the person can be reached by another channel. |
| *Failed … (Meta 131049)* | Meta held a marketing message back: the person has had their share for now. | Counted as failed; try again another day. |
| *Failed … (Meta 131042)* | Meta could not charge the account. | Fix the payment method in WhatsApp Manager. |
| The run fails: *Meta has no approved template of this name and language* | The template was deleted or renamed in WhatsApp Manager. | Sync from Meta, then choose an approved template. |
| *No value for {{tag}} and the template has no fallback* | That person has no value for the tag. | Give the tag an If Empty value and save (a written template needs a copy, reviewed again). |
| Everyone is *No Consent* | Nobody has a WhatsApp opt-in yet. | Record consent (step 4). |

## What a WhatsApp message costs

Meta charges a delivered template message by its category and the recipient's country; a message that is not delivered is free, and so are replies within 24 hours of the customer's own message. The template editor shows the price of the category in your home country, and a campaign's Estimated Cost is worked out country by country. Settings → Message Costs → WhatsApp Countries decides where WhatsApp goes: numbers in other countries are left out of WhatsApp campaigns, and the campaign's Recipients tab gives them as *Country Not Chosen for WhatsApp*.

## Things to know

- Meta rejects a body that starts or ends with a tag, or that has two tags side by side; the editor says so before you submit.
- Links in a WhatsApp message are not tagged with UTM parameters: Meta sends exactly the words and links it approved.
- A new number may start conversations with only a few hundred people a day until it has a sending history; Settings → Delivery shows the number's daily limit and quality.
- Marketing campaigns keep the workspace's quiet hours for texts.
- Not yet built: drip campaigns and journeys on WhatsApp, POS e-receipts on WhatsApp, a reply inbox, Meta's charge per campaign.

---

# The Email Designer

**Where:** Templates → an email template, or New Template → Email; or a campaign's **Open in the Designer**, which opens it in its own tab with **Back to Campaign** instead. Full screen, with Back to Templates at the top left and the help icon beside the template's name.

## What this page is for

The designer is where an email template is built: the subject line and preheader, and the design as blocks dragged onto a canvas. Everything is edited visually; the designer writes the email's source (MJML, a mark-up made for email) and the server renders the HTML every inbox gets, so what the canvas shows is what is sent. Every save is a version, nothing is overwritten, and a template is a draft until Activate makes it usable by campaigns and journeys.

This chapter is the long form: the workspace piece by piece, every block and setting, and then worked examples for each thing the editor can do. The Templates and Content chapter covers the library, the text message editor and the receipt editor.

## Who can use it

| | |
|---|---|
| Permission | `template.edit` to edit, save and activate. Without it a template opens read-only: the canvas can be browsed and the Preview used, nothing can be changed. |
| Features | Barcode and QR Code blocks need **dynamic_codes**; the Deliverability tab needs **deliverability_tests** and a Mailgun Inspect account: C360's, or your own under Settings. |

## The workspace, piece by piece

| Part | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| The top bar | Back to Templates (**Back to Campaign** when a campaign opened the designer), the template's name and status, the three modes (Content, Preview, Deliverability) and History. | The mode you work in. |
| Subject | The subject line, with a tag picker and an emoji picker beside it; the counter turns amber past 60 characters (inboxes cut it there) and the field stops at 300; a hint appears when a word is one spam filters dislike. | The subject every campaign on this template sends, unless the campaign writes its own. Empty, it blocks Activate. |
| Preheader | The text an inbox shows after the subject, with the same pickers; amber past 90 characters. | Hidden text at the top of the email that inboxes show in the list. Optional. |
| Blocks (left rail) | The palette: Structure, Content, Brand and Commerce blocks, with a search. Drag a tile onto the canvas: a copy of the tile follows the pointer until you drop it, and a blue line shows where it will land. Or focus a tile with the arrow keys and press Enter to add it below the selected block. | The design. |
| Assets | The workspace's image library: upload (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, up to 5 MB), search, and pick a picture for the selected Image block, or drag a picture straight onto the canvas. The same file uploaded twice is kept once. | The images the design uses. |
| Layers | The tree of what is on the canvas: sections, columns and blocks, with their names. Select, rename, reorder, hide on canvas. | A quick way to reach a block buried in a layout. |
| Tags | The workspace's personalization tags with their sample values. A tag inserts at the cursor while text is being edited; otherwise it is copied. | `{{tag}}` tokens filled in per recipient at send time. |
| The canvas | The email as it renders, desktop, tablet or phone width, with the selected block outlined and its breadcrumb (section → column → block) above. Click a block to select it, drag its move handle to move it (its name follows the pointer), double-click text to edit it with the formatting toolbar. | |
| The canvas toolbar | Undo, Redo, Zoom In, Zoom Out, Fit to Width, Desktop / Tablet / Mobile, Show Block Outlines, Preview, Fullscreen, View Code, Download HTML. | How you look at the design; View Code and Download HTML hand it to someone else. |
| The settings panel (right) | The selected block's settings in three groups: Content, Layout, Appearance. Every field is listed under the block types below. | The block. |
| The footer | The note: errors that must be fixed, warnings that are advisory, or No errors; **Save**; **Activate** (draft only, with the reason when it cannot); and, when a campaign opened the designer, **Save & Return to Campaign** (*Return to Campaign* when the template cannot be changed). | Save & Return saves the changes (when there are any), closes this tab and brings the campaign back in front, its preview refreshed. If the browser does not let the tab close, the campaign opens in this tab. A save that is refused keeps you here and says why. |
| Errors and Links pills | The findings on the design, each leading to the field or block concerned; every link in the design with its address and what is wrong with it. | |

## Starting a template

**New Template → Email** asks for a name and a **Starting Point**: Blank, or one of C360's starters, which become version 1 of your template and are yours to change:

| Starter | What it holds |
|---|---|
| Welcome email | Brand header, a hero with a greeting, one button, footer. |
| Sale announcement | A headline and offer, a coupon block, a button, footer. |
| Monthly newsletter | Header, a lead story, two columns of stories, footer with social links. |
| Loyalty statement | The member's tier and points from tags, a reward block. |
| Win-back offer | A short note, an offer, a button. |

**Duplicate** on the library's row makes a draft copy of any template's latest version, which is the usual way to make a seasonal variant.

## The blocks

| Group | Block | What it is | Settings that matter |
|---|---|---|---|
| Structure | One, Two, Three, Four Columns | A section with that many columns. Everything else goes into a column. | Section: Email Width, Padding, Full Width, Background Color, Corner Radius, and on the phone canvas the stacking (On Phones: stack, reverse, Keep Side by Side). Column: Width, Padding, Vertical Alignment, Background Color, Corner Radius. |
| Structure | Spacer, Divider | Empty vertical space; a line. | Height; Width, Alignment, Line Color, Line Width. |
| Content | Heading, Text | Rich text with the toolbar: bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, a link, a tag, an emoji. | Alignment, Padding, Font Family, Font Size, Font Weight, Line Height, Text Color, Background Color (the cell behind the text). A link inside text gets Link Color and Underline of its own. |
| Content | Image | A picture from the image library or uploaded now (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, up to 5 MB). Dropped from the palette it is an empty placeholder; **Select Image** on it opens the library. The placeholder is on the canvas only: until a picture is chosen the email has no image there, and the block is listed as a placeholder image. | Image, Alt Text (describe it for people who cannot see it; a missing alt is a finding), Link, Hover Text, Alignment, Width, Height, Max Height, Padding, Corner Radius. |
| Content | Button | A call to action. | Label, Link, Alignment, Width, Height, Margin (space outside), Padding (space inside), Font, Background Color, Text Color, Corner Radius. |
| Content | Custom HTML | Raw HTML you write, for things the blocks cannot do. It is sent as written, so keep to email-safe tables. | HTML, Width, Alignment, Margin, Padding, Background Color. |
| Brand | Header | Your wordmark, an optional tagline, on a brand bar. | Wordmark Text, Tagline, Alignment, Brand Bar Color. |
| Brand | Social Links | Icons for the networks you add, in a row or a column, with or without labels. | Add a Network, then per network: Network (Facebook, Instagram, X, Twitter, LinkedIn, YouTube, TikTok, Pinterest, Snapchat, Google, GitHub, Medium, Tumblr, Vimeo, SoundCloud, Dribbble, Xing, Website), Link, Label; Layout, Icon Size, Icon Padding, Icon Background, Icon Corner Radius, Label Font, Label Size, Label Color. |
| Brand | Footer | The address, the unsubscribe link and the small print. | Sender Address and the legal text. The unsubscribe link must stay for marketing mail. |
| Commerce | Product Card | Image, name, price and a button for one product. | Image with Alt Text, Ratio, the text, the button's Label and Destination. |
| Commerce | Coupon | A headline, body text and a treatment for an offer, with the coupon tags ready to use. | Headline, Body (with quick tags for the coupon code), Alignment, Treatment. |
| Commerce | Barcode, QR Code | A code drawn per recipient from a tag's value, the coupon code uploaded with the list for example. | Source tag; barcode Format (Code 128, Code 39, EAN-13, UPC-A), Width, Height; QR Error Correction (Low, Standard, High, Maximum), Size; the value printed under the code; Alignment; Background. |

Settings every block shares, in Layout: **Margin** (space outside the block), **Padding** (space inside), **Hide on Phones** and **Hide on Desktop**. In Appearance, where the block allows it: Background Color, Border Width, Border Style, Border Color, Shadow (Outlook for Windows drops shadows; the field says so), Corner Radius. Lengths take px, %, em and rem and settle on px.

## Personalization

- Tags come from the workspace's catalogue (Settings → Tags): first name, last name, loyalty tier and so on, the three coupon tags, and the system tags that are always there: unsubscribe URL, preference centre URL, current year, today, the campaign's name, the recipient's email.
- Insert one from the toolbar while editing text, from the Tags panel, or from the picker beside Subject and Preheader. Type it as `{{first_name}}` if you prefer.
- The canvas and the Preview show each tag's sample value; the sent email has the recipient's own, or the tag's default when the person has none. A tag that is not in the catalogue is a finding on Save.
- The coupon tags (`coupon_code`, `coupon_barcode_value`, `coupon_qr_value`) are filled from columns of the same names in the contact list's upload, so an offer can be unique per person.

## Preview mode

| Control | What it is |
|---|---|
| Preview As | Sample Values (the catalogue's samples), Full Profile (a complete customer), Empty Profile (a customer with nothing filled in, to see the defaults). |
| Device | Desktop (680 px) or Mobile (375 px). |
| Client | Generic, Gmail, Outlook, Apple Mail: the inbox row above the email shows the subject and preheader cut where that client cuts them, and the email is rendered with that client's quirks. |
| Appearance | Light or Dark: how the email looks in a dark-mode inbox. |
| Send a Test | The email, as previewed, to your own address or the addresses you type. |

## Deliverability mode

Run Inbox Previews renders the last saved version in real email clients through C360's Mailgun Inspect account, or the workspace's own one under Settings → Sending Engines → Deliverability Testing. Every test of the template is listed on the left; a result shows the renders grouped by client kind, a render opens full size. Through C360's account, tests count against the plan's monthly number plus any tests added for the month, which Settings → Plan & Usage shows; through your own, they are not limited. Unsaved changes are not in a test: save first.

## Saving, versions and activating

- **Save** writes version n+1. The designer also saves a draft by itself 30 seconds after the last edit, unless the template is read-only, an older version is on the canvas, or the last save was refused.
- **History** lists every version with who saved it and when. Open one to look at it: the strip says *You are looking at version n*; saving from there makes it the newest version, nothing is lost; Back to Latest returns.
- **A conflict**: when someone else saved a newer version, the strip says so and offers Load Latest Version. Nothing is sent until you do.
- **Activate** runs the blockers: a subject line, the design saved at least once, an unsubscribe link for marketing mail, images with an alt text and no placeholder images, the HTML under Gmail's 102 KB clip limit. A template with an error cannot be activated; warnings are advisory. Once active, campaigns can pick it on their Content step and journeys on a message step.
- **Leaving** with unsaved changes asks first.
- **Opened from a campaign**: Save & Return to Campaign saves and goes back to the campaign in one step. Every save, from either button, also tells the campaign's tab, which shows the new version without a reload.

## How to use the editor: worked examples

Each example is a complete recipe. Names, colours and addresses are placeholders for your own.

### 1. A welcome email from a starter

1. Templates → New Template → Email → name it *Welcome to Northwind* → Starting Point: Welcome email → Create.
2. Subject: type *Welcome,* then insert `first_name` from the tag picker beside the field; the counter stays green. Preheader: *Your first order ships free this month.*
3. Select the Header block → Wordmark Text: Northwind Apparel → Brand Bar Color: your navy.
4. Double-click the hero text → write the welcome in two short sentences; keep the heading one line.
5. Select the Button → Label: Start Shopping → Link: your shop's address; Appearance → Background Color: your action colour.
6. Preview → Client: Gmail → check the inbox row reads well where Gmail cuts it; Device: Mobile → check the button is reachable with a thumb.
7. Send a Test to yourself, then Save and Activate. The campaign's Content step now offers it.

### 2. Formatting text, links and emoji

1. Double-click a Text block. The toolbar appears above it: **bold**, *italic*, underline, strikethrough, link, tag, emoji.
2. Select a phrase and press the link button → paste the address. Select the link again: the settings panel now shows **Link Color** and **Underline** for that link alone, so a link can be your brand blue without an underline while the paragraph stays black.
3. Press the tag button → pick `first_name`: it lands where the cursor is. Press the emoji button for a mark at the cursor; the emoji picker beside Subject does the same for the subject line.
4. The block's own settings set the whole block: Font Family (the email fonts the workspace offers; the default is the design's own), Font Size, Font Weight, Line Height, Text Color, Alignment. Use the toolbar for a word, the panel for the block.
5. Press Escape to finish editing. The **Links** pill at the bottom lists every link in the design with its address: open it before saving to catch a link to nowhere.

### 3. A hero: a coloured section, a picture and a button

1. Drag **One Column** (Structure) below the header. Select the section (the breadcrumb above the canvas shows section → column → block; click *section*).
2. Appearance → Background Color: your brand colour; Layout → Full Width on, so the colour runs edge to edge in inboxes that allow it; Padding: 32 px top and bottom.
3. Drag an **Image** into the column → **Select Image** on the placeholder → upload the hero picture → Alt Text: what it shows (*A rack of autumn coats*). Width: 100%; Corner Radius: 8 px.
4. Drag a **Heading** under the image → Text Color: white; then a **Button** → Label: Shop the Collection → Link; Corner Radius: 8 px; Margin 16 px on top to lift it off the heading.
5. Switch the canvas to **Mobile**: the picture scales to the column; if it is too tall, set **Max Height** on the image. Save.

### 4. Columns that behave on a phone

1. Drag **Two Columns** → left column an Image, right column a Heading and a Text. On the desktop canvas they sit side by side.
2. Switch to **Mobile**. Columns stack, picture first. If the words should come first on a phone, select the section → Layout → **On Phones: reverse**.
3. Two small items that should stay beside each other on a phone (two icons, a price and a button): select their section → **Keep Side by Side**. They share the width instead of stacking.
4. A **Three Columns** section with a Product Card in each stacks to three cards on a phone; if that is too long, select the third card → Layout → **Hide on Phones**. The desktop email keeps all three.
5. The other way round: a square banner that only phones should see → set its section to **Hide on Desktop**. The canvas hides it on the device it does not apply to; Show Block Outlines on the toolbar keeps hidden blocks findable.

### 5. Images: the library, alt text and sizes

1. Select an Image block → **Image** → the library opens: search by name, pick one, or Upload (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, up to 5 MB; a GIF animates in inboxes that play them, Outlook shows its first frame). The same file uploaded twice is kept once, so re-uploading is harmless.
2. **Alt Text** is read by people who cannot see the picture and shown where images are blocked; a picture without one is a finding, and a placeholder picture blocks Activate.
3. **Link** makes the picture clickable; **Hover Text** is the tooltip. **Width** in %, **Max Height** to keep tall pictures in check, **Corner Radius** for rounded corners, **Padding** for breathing room.
4. Give photos a 2:1 or 3:2 shape; a 600 px wide export is plenty for a 680 px email and keeps the message under Gmail's clip limit.

### 6. A sale with a personal coupon and a barcode

1. New Template → Email → Starting Point: Sale announcement.
2. Drag a **Coupon** block under the hero. Headline: *20% off this weekend*; Body: insert the `coupon_code` tag from the quick tags, so each person sees their own code; Treatment: dashed.
3. Drag a **Barcode** block under the coupon. Source: `coupon_barcode_value`; Format: Code 128; the value under the code on. The canvas shows the sample value's bars; the sent email draws each recipient's.
4. Upload the contact list with columns named `coupon_code` and `coupon_barcode_value`; the list's people carry them.
5. A recipient with no code gets the email without the barcode block; the run's completion message says how many.
6. Preview As → Empty Profile shows what a person without a code sees. Save, Activate.

### 7. A QR code for in-store redemption

1. Drag a **QR Code** block (Commerce; offered when the workspace has Barcodes and QR codes). Source: `coupon_qr_value`; Error Correction: Standard (High if the code will be printed small or on a dark background); Size: 160 px; Alignment: centre.
2. Give the QR value in the list upload a full URL or the till's code, whatever the store scans.
3. On a dark section, set the block's **Background** to white: a QR code needs light around it to scan.

### 8. Social links, including TikTok

1. Drag **Social Links** (Brand) into the footer section. **Add a Network** → Network: Instagram → Link: your profile. Add Facebook, TikTok, YouTube the same way; each row has its own Link and an optional Label.
2. Layout: a row for a footer, a column for a sidebar. Icon Size 32 px, Icon Background your brand colour or none, Icon Corner Radius 16 px for round icons; Label Font, Label Size, Label Color when labels are shown. The TikTok mark takes the colour its neighbours have.

### 9. Something the blocks cannot do: Custom HTML

1. Drag **Custom HTML** (Content) where it should go: into a column, a section or the body. It refuses to land inside another raw block.
2. HTML: write an email-safe table, for example a two-row opening-hours table with inline styles. No scripts and no external stylesheets: the server rejects scripts, and inboxes ignore stylesheets.
3. Layout: Width (full or a fixed width), Alignment, Margin and Padding, Background Color: these wrap your HTML in a table the inboxes understand, so the block lines up with the rest. Hide on Phones and Hide on Desktop work on it too.
4. Preview in Outlook as well as Gmail: raw HTML is where the two differ most.

### 10. Spacing with rhythm

1. Put a **Spacer** (Structure) between sections and tune its Height once, rather than padding every block: one place to change the rhythm.
2. On a block, **Margin** is the space outside it (between it and its neighbours), **Padding** the space inside (between its edge and its content). A Button is the one block where Padding is the space inside the button itself.
3. The four boxes take px or %, and em or rem if you think that way: 1.5em becomes px the moment you press Enter, so the email stays predictable in every inbox. Mixing px and % on one side is refused.

### 11. Borders and shadows

1. Select a section, column, image or button → Appearance → Border Width 1 px, Border Style solid, Border Color a light grey: a hairline card. Corner Radius rounds it.
2. Shadow: soft, medium or strong on a section, column, text, image or button. Apple Mail, Gmail and Outlook.com draw it; Outlook for Windows drops shadows, so never let a shadow carry meaning.

### 12. The subject line and preheader

1. Write the subject as a sentence, under 60 characters: the counter turns amber past that, where inboxes cut it. Insert `first_name` from the picker for a personal subject, an emoji from the picker beside it if your brand uses them.
2. A hint appears under the field when a word is one spam filters weigh (*free*, *act now*, capitals, stacked punctuation). It is advice, not a verdict.
3. The preheader is the line inboxes show after the subject: write the second half of the thought, under 90 characters. Leave it empty and inboxes show the first words of the email instead.
4. Preview → Client: Gmail, Outlook, Apple Mail: the inbox row above the email shows where each cuts the two.

### 13. Checking the email in Preview

1. Switch to **Preview**. Preview As: Sample Values first, then **Empty Profile**: every tag shows its default, which is what a customer with a thin record sees. Fix a default under Settings → Tags.
2. Device: Mobile. Everything should be readable without zooming; buttons wide enough for a thumb.
3. Client: Outlook. Shadows are gone, rounded corners square off; the design should still hold.
4. Appearance: Dark. A dark header with dark text, or a logo with a transparent background on a dark bar, shows here before a customer sees it.
5. **Send a Test** to yourself and a colleague: the real email through the workspace's sender, as the previewed profile.

### 14. Inbox previews in real clients

1. Save, then **Deliverability → Run Inbox Previews**. The last saved version is rendered in real clients through Mailgun Inspect.
2. The result lists renders grouped by kind (desktop, phone, webmail); open one full size. Each test counts against the plan's monthly number unless the workspace uses its own Inspect account.

### 15. Versions: going back, and two people at once

1. **History** at the top right lists every version with who saved it and when. Open one to look at it; the strip says *You are looking at version n*. **Save** from there makes it the newest version, so a design from last month is one Save away. **Back to Latest** returns without saving.
2. When a colleague saves while you are editing, the strip says a newer version exists and offers **Load Latest Version**. Your unsaved work is lost by loading it, so copy any text you wrote first. Nothing is sent until the conflict is resolved.
3. The designer saves a draft by itself 30 seconds after the last edit; the footer says when it last saved.

### 16. Handing the design to a campaign or a journey

1. **Activate**. The footer names anything that blocks it: an empty subject, a placeholder image, an image without alt text, a marketing email without the unsubscribe link, an email over Gmail's 102 KB limit. Fix and Activate again.
2. A campaign picks the template on its Content step and may write its own subject and preheader without touching the template. A journey's Email step picks it in the step's panel.
3. Changing an active template changes what campaigns send from the next save that is activated; the campaigns themselves need no edit.

### 17. Taking the design elsewhere

1. **View Code** on the canvas toolbar shows the MJML source and the rendered HTML, formatted, with a copy button: for an agency, another tool, or a quick look at what an inbox will receive.
2. **Download HTML** saves the rendered email as a file.
3. **Fullscreen** hides the rails for a clean look at the canvas; **Exit Fullscreen** brings them back. **Fit to Width** and the zoom menu size the canvas to the window.

### 18. Finding and fixing a buried block

1. Open **Layers**: the tree of sections, columns and blocks with their names. Click one to select it on the canvas; rename it so the tree reads like the email; drag to reorder; the eye hides it on the canvas while you work (it still sends).
2. The breadcrumb above the canvas walks up from a block to its column and section, which is the quickest way to select a section to change its background or stacking.
3. The **Errors** pill lists every finding with a link to the field or block concerned.

## Things to know

- The subject and preheader on the template are defaults: a campaign can write its own on its Content step without touching the template.
- Marketing mail must carry the unsubscribe link; the Footer block has it. Remove it and Activate refuses, and a marketing campaign cannot publish.
- Images are stored once per workspace and served from the media store; the same file uploaded twice is kept once.
- Email clients differ: Outlook for Windows has no shadows and no media queries (the phone rules use its own mechanism), Gmail clips emails over 102 KB. The Preview's clients show the common cases; Deliverability shows the real ones.
- A design made outside C360 can be pasted as Custom HTML, but the blocks cannot edit what is inside it; rebuild with blocks where you will want to change it later.
- A text template has no designer: SMS and MMS open the message editor, e-receipts the receipt editor.

---

# Setting Up a New Workspace

**Where:** Settings → General → Set-Up. The first sign-in of an administrator lands here while the workspace is not ready, and Home keeps a card pointing back until it is.

## What this is

The platform team creates the workspace and sends its first administrator an email with a one-time password link and the set-up list. From that email to the first production send is one path, and this chapter walks it: choosing a password, the first sign-in, the Set-Up page that knows what is done, the steps in order, a rehearsal with a test campaign, and the first real send. Nothing in it is a new form: every step opens the Settings page that does it, and those pages have their own chapters.

## Who does it

| | |
|---|---|
| Permission | `user.admin`. The first administrator holds the Workspace Admin role, which has every permission including `user.admin`. Anyone with `user.admin` sees the Set-Up page and the Home card. |
| Features | The texts step reads *Texts are not switched on for this workspace* without the **sms** feature; the API Keys button on the last Engagement step needs **api_access**. The platform team switches features on per workspace. |

## The path, from the email

### Before the first sign-in

1. **The email.** It names the workspace and its code (for example `NWA`), carries the password link and lists the set-up steps. The link is one-time: if it was used or has expired, ask the platform team, or any administrator who already has access, for a new one (Settings → Users → Password Link).
2. **Choose a password.** The link opens the password page; choose one and continue to the sign-in.
3. **Sign in.** The Workspace ID (already filled in when you open the workspace's own address), your email and the password you chose for this workspace. When the workspace requires multi-factor authentication, the next screen walks you through it before anything else: scan the code with an authenticator app, type the six digits, store the recovery codes.
4. **You land on Set-Up.** An administrator whose workspace is not ready lands here instead of Home: *Welcome, {name}. Let's set up {CODE}.* with step 1 already done. Everyone else, and every later sign-in, lands on Home, where a card says how far the set-up is until the workspace is ready.

### The steps, in order

Set-Up lists only the steps of the components the workspace has: steps 1 and 2 for every workspace, six more when it has Engagement, and **Connect the database your reports read** when it has Insights. A workspace with Engagement alone sees eight steps, one with both sees nine, and one with Insights alone sees three (sign in, team, database), where the database step is then required. The numbers below are for a workspace with Engagement.

| # | Step | What to do | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Choose your password and sign in | Done by reading this. Account Security sets up your own second step. | You are signed in. |
| 2 | Invite your team and give them roles | Users → Add User: email, name, a role. Three roles are ready: Workspace Admin, Campaign Manager, Analyst; Roles adds narrower ones, for example an approver. Each person gets their own password link. | At least one other active user with a role. |
| 3 | Add an email engine | Sending Engines → Add Engine: Postal or SendGrid with its address and key → Check. Then Copy Webhook URL and register the address with the provider, so deliveries and bounces come back. | An active email engine whose last Check passed. |
| 4 | Add an email sender and verify it | Sending Engines → Add Sender on the engine: the From name and address on a domain the engine may send for → Verify; the message arrives in your own inbox. Tick Default. | A verified email sender on an active email engine. |
| 5 | Warm up your domain and IP (optional) | Delivery Warm-Up → choose how mail leaves, the plan, the lists, the template and the sender → Start Warm-Up. For a new IP address or sending domain: it sends a little more each day and holds every marketing email to the day's limit until it is done. | A warm-up finished, or one that was stopped. Optional. |
| 6 | Add a text engine and a sender number (optional) | Sending Engines → Add Engine: Digivize with its keys and brand, or the Sandbox to rehearse without an aggregator → a sender with the number → Verify by a text. | An active text engine with a verified number. Optional. |
| 7 | Import your do-not-contact list, then your first contact list | Suppressions → Import the opt-outs from your previous system first, so no upload can reach them. Then Contact Lists → Create List → Upload a File: name it, say what it is for, match the columns, declare where the people agreed, review, import. | An active production list with people. |
| 8 | Decide who is told, and who approves (optional) | Notifications: the addresses or chat webhook that hear about failed sends. Approvals: whether a second person signs off before a production publish. API Keys: a key with `receipts:send` when the point of sale will send receipts. | A notification address, or approvals on, or an API key. Optional. |
| 9 (Insights) | Connect the database your reports read (optional with Engagement) | Report Databases → Connect a Database: a read-only login to the database the reports read. | A Report Database is connected. |

**Ready** means every step not marked optional is done: steps 1 to 4 and 7 with Engagement; steps 1 to 3 with Insights alone. The Home card goes, and the page stays under Settings with the optional steps still listed.

### Before the first real send

Worth setting while the workspace is quiet, each with its own chapter:

- **Consent Policy** (Settings → Engagement): what an upload does with people without a consent date, whether text lists need a text consent date, approval before a fresh list's first send, and the sentence uploaders confirm.
- **Preference Centre**: switch on when customers should choose topics; add the topics first and put `{{preference_url}}` in the templates' footer.
- **Email Server**: your own SMTP server for password links and notifications, so they come from your domain.
- **Tags**: the workspace's own personalization tags beyond the platform's catalogue.
- **Security**: require multi-factor authentication for everyone, or sign in through your identity provider.
- **Templates**: copy a preset, put your brand on it, Activate.

### The rehearsal, then production

1. **A test campaign.** Campaigns → Create Campaign → Regular Marketing, Email, Marketing → a Test list (Contact Lists lets you make one from a file of colleagues) → the template → Send a Test to yourself → Test Publish. A test publish sends to nobody real and counts in nothing.
2. **The first production send.** The same campaign with the production list attached, the sender verified, a subject line, the topic when the preference centre is on → Review & Publish → Production Publish. The checklist on that step names anything missing.
3. **Watch it land.** The campaign's Performance tab and Recipients show each message's story as the engine calls back; Home's Needs Attention raises a failed run; Settings → Integrations keeps every call.

## The Set-Up page's controls

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| The heading | *Welcome, {name}. Let's set up {CODE}.* while only step 1 is done; *Set Up Your Workspace* afterwards. Under it the workspace's name and code, its plan and its time zone, and on a brand-new workspace how many steps there are (with Engagement: *Nothing sends until step 4*). | Nothing; it tells you at a glance whether the set-up has started. |
| Hide Until Later / Show on Home Again | Shown only while the workspace is not ready. Hide Until Later hides the set-up card; Show on Home Again brings it back. | For the whole workspace, not only for you: hidden, Home shows no set-up card and an administrator's first sign-in lands on Home instead of here. The page and its steps stay under Settings. |
| n of N done, the meter | How many of the listed steps are done, optional ones included. | Nothing; the meter fills as steps are done anywhere in the workspace. |
| A step's row | Its number (blue for the next required step, amber when it needs attention, a tick when done), its name, an *Optional* tag where it applies, and a sentence read from the workspace, for example the engine's name and when it was last checked. The next step also shows why it matters. | Nothing on its own; it reports what the workspace already has. Each row updates when the page that does the work is saved. |
| The status tag | **Done** (green) when the step is done. Otherwise an amber tag for a step that needs attention: **Not checked** (the email engine's Check has not passed), **Not verified** (a sender exists but is not verified: test sends work from it, production does not), **No sender** (a text engine without a verified number). The warm-up step shows **Day n of m** while it runs, **Paused**, **Waiting for Approval** when the next day waits to be approved, and **Stopped** (green, counted as done) when someone stopped it. | Nothing; it names what is missing. |
| The buttons on a row | The pages that do the step, for example Sending Engines, Verify Sender, Delivery Warm-Up, Upload a List. The main one is blue on the next step. | They open the page; nothing is changed until you save there. |
| The callout | Which steps make the workspace ready (*When steps 1 to 4 and 7 are done, the workspace is ready*), or *Your workspace is ready*. | Nothing; it tells you which steps hold the workspace back. |

## What happens behind the scenes

Every load reads the users and roles, the engines and their last check, the senders, the warm-up, the suppressions and lists, the notification settings, the approval switch, the API keys and, with Insights, the report databases, and decides each step from them; nothing is ticked by hand. Hiding the Home card is the only thing the page stores. The platform team sees the same readiness from their side, so they know when a workspace is sending.

## Things to know

- Until an email engine has been checked and a sender verified, test sends work and production publishing does not.
- A password link is one-time and expires (72 hours unless the platform team set another length).
- A warm-up that is still running is not done, but it never holds the workspace back: it is optional. While it runs, every marketing email waits for the hour's limit.
- The optional steps never hold the workspace back, and the page keeps listing them until they are done.

---

# Sending Engines and Senders

**Where:** Settings → Engagement → Sending Engines.

## What this page is for

Nothing leaves C360 without an engine. An engine is the service that actually delivers your messages: a Postal or SendGrid account for email, Digivize for texts, your own mail server, or the Sandbox that sends nothing while you try things out. A sender is the name and address (or the number) your messages come from, on one of those engines. This page is where both are added, checked and kept healthy, and where the engine is told how to report deliveries back to C360.

A campaign picks a sender when it is set up; the sender decides the engine. Until a workspace has a checked email engine and a verified sender, test sends work but nothing can be published to production.

## Who can use it

| | |
|---|---|
| Permission | `provider.edit`. Without it the page says so and names the permission to ask for. Settings as a whole needs `user.admin`. |
| Features | Email engines are always offered. Text engines (Digivize, Sandbox) need the **sms** feature; MMS needs **mms** as well. The platform team switches features on per workspace. |

## The fields and controls

### The engines table

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Provider | The engine's name, its channel tag (Email, Texts or WhatsApp) and **Default** when it is the one new senders are offered first. Under it, the engine type and its address, and *separate key for receipts* when a second credential is stored. | Nothing by itself; it is the row's identity. |
| Health | What C360 found when it last checked the engine (every 10 minutes): **Healthy**, **Attention**, **Failing**, **Quiet** (nothing sent in the last day, so nothing to judge) or **No Receipts Yet** (a text engine that has never sent a delivery receipt). Under the tag, the reason in one line, or when it was checked; **See Health** opens the checks. Before the first check the column shows the engine's last Check: **Not Checked Yet**, **Checked** with the date and time, or **Check Failed**. **Switched Off** when *In Use* is unticked. | Nothing by itself: it tells you what to fix. The platform team is alerted when an engine is Failing or needs attention. Set-Up counts an email engine as done only when its Check passed. A switched-off engine is skipped by every send. |
| Callbacks | **Copy Webhook URL** copies the engine's callback address, the address the provider must post delivery results to. **Rotate Address** makes a fresh address and retires the old one at once. Under them: when the last callback arrived and how many came in the last 24 hours, or a warning that none ever has. | Without a registered callback address C360 sends but never learns what happened: no delivered, bounced, opened or clicked counts, and bounced addresses are not blocked. Rotating means registering the new address with the provider again. |
