Every report asks these filters above its rows, first and in this order, whether you made it yourself or with AI Reports; your own filters follow them:

- **Campaign** (data with a campaign): pick one or several campaigns. Left empty, it shows the **latest campaign** sent in the period, and says which ("Latest: Hottest Designer Deals Oct 17 Day 2"). If none was sent in the period, it shows every campaign of the period.
- **Channel** (data with a channel): one or several channels; left empty, every channel.
- **Period** (data with a date): **Last 7 Days** (the default), Last 15 Days, Last 30 Days, Last Quarter (3 months), Last Half Year (6 months), Last Year (12 months) or **Custom Range** with two dates. The days it covers show under it. Period is always asked, so no report reads a whole table unless you ask for a longer span; it filters on the data's date and time, which the database's index can use.

In the editor, **Report Filters** lists them with Period at the top: change its label or its default, but it cannot be removed. Campaign and Channel are ordinary filters: change or remove them. A draft from AI Reports lists them under **Asks** before you open it. On a dashboard, a tile uses the dashboard's own period instead of Period; a schedule or an alert uses the report's default (7 days unless you changed it). Reports made before were given the filters by the platform team (`manage.py insights_report_filters --tenant CODE --apply`), each as a new version noted "Report filters added"; until then a report shows and runs with them anyway.

**Set your fiscal year (Settings → Fiscal Year)** (workspace administrators)

1. Open Settings → **Fiscal Year** and choose the month it **Starts In**, such as April. It saves at once.
2. Choose whether to **Name a Fiscal Year By** the year it ends in (April 2026 to March 2027 is FY2027) or the year it starts in (FY2026). The page shows an example.
3. In the editor, a date then also groups **by Fiscal Quarter** and **by Fiscal Year**, read as FY2027 Q1. Filters offer **in this fiscal year**, **in last fiscal year**, **in this fiscal quarter** and **in last fiscal quarter**.
4. **Year to Date** and **Quarter to Date** comparisons count from the fiscal year's first day. Dashboards offer **Fiscal Quarter** and **Fiscal Year** periods (to date).

**Work out a value for each row with a formula** (anyone who can build reports)

1. In the editor, press **Custom Fields**, then **Add a Custom Field**. Give it a **Name** and choose the **Kind** **Formula (Each Row)**.
2. Write the formula. Put a field in square brackets, such as `[Revenue] - [Cost]`, or click a field under **Fields** to put it in where the cursor is. Click a function under **Functions** to put it in, such as `IF(`.
3. The line under the formula checks it as you type. It says what it works out and which fields it reads, or what is wrong, with the place marked.
4. A number formula is **A Measure (Adds Up)** or **A Dimension (Groups)**: choose a dimension for a year or a code. Choose how a number shows under **Show As**: Whole Number, Decimal, Money or Percentage.
5. Press **Add Field**. The formula is then a field like any other: drag it into Rows or Values, filter on it, or group a date formula by month.

Examples: `IF([Amount] >= 500, "Gold", "Standard")`, `YEAR([Sent]) & "-" & TEXT(MONTH([Sent]))`, `DATEDIFF([Ordered], TODAY(), "day")`, `COALESCE([Discount], 0)`.

**Write a measure formula, such as a rate or a filtered total**

1. In **Custom Fields**, add a field of the **Kind** **Measure Formula**.
2. A measure formula adds up the rows of each group, so every field goes inside SUM, AVERAGE, MIN, MAX, COUNT or DISTINCTCOUNT, or you count rows with COUNTROWS().
3. Give an aggregate a condition to add up only some rows: `SUM([Sent Count], [Channel] = "EMAIL")` is the email sends of each group.
4. Divide safely with DIVIDE: `DIVIDE(SUM([Delivered Count]), SUM([Sent Count])) * 100` is empty, not an error, where nothing was sent. Choose **Percentage** under **Show As**.
5. A measure formula may use the dataset's calculated measures and the measure formulas above it by name, such as `[Delivered Rate] / 100`.
6. Add it to **Values**: it is worked out for every row, in the totals, the pivot and the charts. Comparisons (previous period, last year) work on it as on any measure.

**The formula language at a glance**

- Fields: `[Field Name]` (the name you see; the field's key also works). Text: `"EMAIL"` or `'EMAIL'`. Yes and no: `TRUE`, `FALSE`. Empty: `BLANK`.
- Operators: `+ - * /` (dividing by zero gives an empty value), `&` joins text, `= <> < <= > >=` compare, `AND`, `OR`, `NOT`.
- Adding up (measure formulas only): SUM, AVERAGE, MIN, MAX, COUNT, DISTINCTCOUNT, each with an optional condition, and COUNTROWS(condition). SUMX, AVERAGEX, MINX, MAXX and COUNTX are the same, for a value worked out per row: `SUMX([Quantity] * [Price])`.
- Share, rank and time (measure formulas only): CALCULATE with ALL, RANKX, TOTALYTD, TOTALQTD, SAMEPERIODLASTYEAR, PREVIOUSPERIOD, PARALLELPERIOD (see the steps below).
- Naming parts: `VAR Sent = SUM([Sent Count]) VAR Delivered = SUM([Delivered Count]) RETURN DIVIDE(Delivered, Sent) * 100`.
- Logic: IF, SWITCH, ISBLANK, COALESCE. Numbers: DIVIDE, ROUND, ABS, FLOOR, CEILING, INT, MOD, POWER, SQRT, EXP, LN, LOG, SIGN, VALUE (text to a number).
- Text: CONCAT, UPPER, LOWER, TRIM, LEFT, RIGHT, MID, LEN, FIND, CONTAINS (any case), REPLACE (or SUBSTITUTE), TEXT.
- Dates: YEAR, QUARTER, MONTH, DAY, WEEKDAY (1 is Sunday), WEEKNUM, HOUR (in your workspace's time zone), EOMONTH, DATE(year, month, day), TODAY, NOW, DATEDIFF(start, end, "day" | "month" | "year"), DATEADD(date, number, "day" | "month" | "year").
- A formula can use the custom fields above it in the list, and the dataset's shared formulas. A formula that reads personal data is personal too.

**Show a share of the total, a rank, year to date or last year in a measure formula**

1. In **Custom Fields**, add a **Measure Formula**.
2. A share of the parent: `DIVIDE(SUM([Sent Count]), CALCULATE(SUM([Sent Count]), ALL([Channel]))) * 100` is each channel's part of its month's sends when the report shows Sent by Month and Channel. ALL names the fields to look past; `ALL()` looks past all of them, for a share of the grand total. Choose **Percentage** under **Show As**.
3. CALCULATE also narrows: `CALCULATE(SUM([Amount]), [Kind] = "REFUND")` adds up the refunds only.
4. A rank: `RANKX(SUM([Amount]))` numbers the rows from the largest, 1 first; `RANKX(SUM([Amount]), "asc")` from the smallest. RANKX is a measure formula of its own; it does not go inside a sum.
5. Time: put a date in the report's Rows, such as Ordered by Month. Then `TOTALYTD(SUM([Amount]))` is the year to date (from your fiscal year's first day), `TOTALQTD(...)` the quarter to date, `SAMEPERIODLASTYEAR(SUM([Amount]))` the same month a year before, `PREVIOUSPERIOD(SUM([Amount]))` the month before, and `PARALLELPERIOD(SUM([Amount]), -3, "month")` three months before. Mix them: `SUM([Amount]) - SAMEPERIODLASTYEAR(SUM([Amount]))` is the change on last year.
6. The line under the formula says when a formula is worked out over the report's rows. Such a formula shows in each row; the report's totals row leaves it empty.
7. The report keeps its date filter: last year's figures are read even when the report shows only this year.

**Share a formula with every report on a dataset** (anyone who can build reports)

1. Open **Insights**, then **Shared Formulas**. The list shows every dataset's formulas, with a **Dataset** column; the **Dataset** chip narrows it to one, and **Kind** and **Certified** narrow it further.
2. Press **Add a Formula**. Step 1, **About the Formula**: the **Dataset** (set from the chip), **Measure Formula** or **Formula (Each Row)**, a **Name** and a **Description** that says what it means.
3. Step 2, **The Formula**: write it as in Custom Fields, with the fields and functions beside it. It may use the dataset's fields and the shared formulas above it. Press **Add Formula**. **Edit** on a formula opens on this step.
4. Every report on that dataset now lists it among the fields. It shows the word **Shared** next to its name, or a tick when it is certified.
5. To share a formula you wrote in a report: in the report's **Custom Fields**, press **Share With Every Report** next to it; the pop-up opens on The Formula, filled in; press **Add Formula**. The report then uses the shared formula in place of its own.
6. A formula that reports use cannot be removed: the **Reports** column shows how many. Renaming one asks first, because a report may call it by name.

**Certify a shared formula** (report administrators)

1. In **Shared Formulas**, open the **…** menu on a formula and press **Certify**. It shows **Certified**, with your name and the time when you point at the mark.
2. Only a report administrator changes or removes a certified formula. Anyone else who needs a different version saves it under another name.
3. Press **Remove Certification** to take the mark off.

## What Insights is for

Insights is the suite's reporting app. It lets you build your own reports on the data C360 already keeps, your messages, the orders they led to, your contacts and your journeys, see them as a grid, a chart, a hierarchy or a pivot, put them on a dashboard with one period for all of them, export them as CSV, Excel or PDF, and have them emailed on a schedule. Nothing is collected for it; it reads what the rest of the suite records.

## Who can use it

| Permission | What it allows |
|---|---|
| Reports · View (`report.view`) | Open and run the reports and dashboards shared with the workspace, export them, star favourites, switch a report's view. |
| Reports · Edit (`report.edit`) | Make and change your own reports, dashboards, folders and schedules; delete your own. |
| Report Admin (`report.admin`) | Everything on every report, dashboard, folder and schedule in the workspace; build on personal-data fields; see the Run Log. |
| User Admin (`user.admin`) | Also builds on personal-data fields. |

The workspace needs the Insights component switched on by the platform team; without it the menu does not offer Insights.

## The datasets

A report is built on one dataset. Each offers **dimensions** (things to group by, such as Campaign, Channel or Sent month) and **measures** (numbers to total, such as Sent Count or Revenue). The datasets today:

| Dataset | One row per | Typical use |
|---|---|---|
| Messages | Every email and text a campaign sent | Sends, deliveries, opens and clicks by campaign, channel or month. |
| Attributed Orders | Every order the shop reported and the campaign it was credited to | Revenue by campaign, channel, store or month. |
| Contacts | Every contact in the workspace | Contacts by city, region, source or consent. |
| Journey Enrolments | Every customer in a journey | Enrolments, completions and conversions by journey. |

Fields marked **PII** carry personal data (a customer's identifier, an email address). Building on them needs Report Admin or User Admin. When you open a shared report that has such a column and you lack the right, its values are masked and the grid says so.

## Reports

### The list

Insights → Reports lists every report you can open, grouped by its component (the one chosen for it, or Automatic: its data's), with its view, dataset, owner and last run. A report drafted with AI carries an **AI** mark beside its name. Your **favourites** sit first: press the star on any row to add one. **Folders** group a workspace's reports; the chips at the top filter by folder or by No Folder, and the folder shows under the report's name. Search finds a report by name.

### Making a report

1. Press **New Report**, pick a dataset and give it a name. It opens in the editor with the dataset's first columns. Under **Your Databases** are the workspace's own databases to query and the datasets mapped from them; a report author can **Connect a Database** there (MySQL, PostgreSQL or ClickHouse, read-only; see Report Databases). Under **Your Files**, **Upload a File** adds a CSV or Excel file.
2. Build it with one of the two editors, switched at the top right and remembered for you:
   - **Field Wells**: the dataset's fields on the left, the result in the middle, the wells on the right. Click or drag a field into **Rows** (one line per value), **Columns** (one dimension spread across, for the pivot and the chart's series) or **Values** (a number to total, with its calculation). **Filters**, then **Sort and Limit**, sit under them.
   - **Steps**: the question written top to bottom as numbered steps: Data, Filter, Summarize, Group By, Sort and Limit. Each step has an Add button; the result sits under the steps.
   A date dimension can be grouped **by Day, Week, Month, Quarter or Year**. A measure has its calculation: Sum, Count, Distinct Count, Average, Min or Max, and under it **Add a Comparison** (the steps are at the top of this chapter).
3. **Filters**: one row per rule (field, operator, value), joined with **and** or **or**. A list is comma-separated, a range is from..to, "in the last days" takes a number. Dates are the workspace's time zone.
4. The preview runs as you build, up to the preview cap. The line above it says how many rows, how long, and how fresh the data is.
5. **Save** writes a new version. Cancel drops the changes.

### Base Reports: ready-made reports from the platform

**Add Base Reports** on Insights → Reports offers the platform's ready-made **packs**: datasets, shared formulas, reports and dashboards that work together. You see the packs your workspace's components fit; one the platform team chose for you when the workspace was set up is marked **Chosen at Onboarding**. A pack is added as this workspace's own copies, filed in a folder named after it; you may change them like any report, and a later version of the pack never changes them. Adding a pack needs report administration.

The first pack is **Email Campaign Reports**, the reports of CmpReport on the mail server's own tables: Campaign KPIs, Campaign Send Status, Delivery Outcomes, Failure and Bounce Reasons, Provider Performance, Retry Analysis, Contact-List Quality, Link Clicks, Opens by Device and Client, Opens over Time, Customer Detail, Unsubscribes, Campaign Comparison and the best and worst campaigns, with a **Management Dashboard** (every campaign in the period) and a **Campaign Performance** dashboard (one campaign, the latest unless you pick another).

1. Press **Add Base Reports**, then **See What's in It** to read a pack's contents, and **Add to** your workspace. A pack that needs a database you have not connected says so, with a link to Report Databases.
2. **Your data**: pick the **Connection** that holds the mail server's tables. The databases on it holding every table the pack reads are listed with their messages and campaign emails; those with campaign mail are ticked. **Campaign Names** offers the tables that hold a campaign's name beside its id; without one, a campaign is named from its subject and first send. **Leave Out** drops campaigns whose name contains any of the words (test sends, client lists). **Unsubscribe Link Contains** tells a click on the unsubscribe link from a click on the content.
3. **Check** runs each dataset once and says how many campaigns were sent in the last 90 days. **Add Reports** then makes everything in one go: if any part does not fit, nothing is added and the message names the part.
4. The last step lists what was made; **Open Campaign Performance** opens the dashboard.

A campaign email is one whose mail-server tag carries a campaign id; e-receipts, which carry one too, are left out. The mail server keeps about two months of mail, so older campaigns drop out of the reports as their mail is removed.

### Ask for a Report, with AI

When an AI provider with a key is switched on (the platform's, or your workspace's own) and your workspace allows AI (Settings → AI Assistant), people who can build reports see **AI Reports** in the Insights menu, above Reports. Its page holds the prompt box, **Run Details** below it and **Prompt History** below that.

**Report Name** at the top of the box names the report; leave it empty and the model names it. The name field on the draft still changes it before you open it.


1. Choose the data with **Ask About** at the top of the box: All My Data, the platform's datasets, one of your Report Databases (with **Also Look at Tables Not Mapped Yet** for its tables nobody has mapped), or your Uploaded Files. The model reads only the fields of your choice. The choice is remembered in your browser.
   Write what you want to see, in your own words: "Revenue by channel and month for the last quarter, as a pivot". The examples under the box are a start. The box takes up to 100,000 characters; put the details on more lines (the filters, the order, what to compare). A counter appears from 90,000.
   **Documents and images:** drop them on the box, press the paperclip at its bottom left, or paste them, to send up to three documents (the report's spec as PDF, Word .docx, Excel .xlsx, CSV, text or Markdown) and up to five images (a sketch of the report you want, or a screenshot of a chart, a table or a spreadsheet) with the prompt. Remove one with its ✕. They go to the model with your prompt and are not kept; only the prompt is saved with the draft.
2. Pick a model at the bottom of the box, beside Generate: Claude or OpenAI, whichever your platform offers, each with a "best for reports" and a "fastest" model. Your last choice is remembered.
3. Press **Generate Report** (or Ctrl+Enter). The model receives your prompt, any documents and images you added and the names of the fields you may use (and, for a database's tables not mapped yet, their names and columns), never customer rows, and answers in one of three ways:
   While it works **Run Details** lists the steps as the server takes them, each ticked as it finishes, the one under way with the time so far; a second step for the model appears when it asks for more tables or its first draft did not fit, and a step that stops says why. The answer shows there too, and the runs before it stay under **Earlier This Visit** (with their time, tokens, model and outcome) while the browser tab is open.
   - **A draft**, in words: its name (a field you can change before you open it), view, data, rows, columns, values and filter, with how many rows the preview gives, what it read, how long it took and its steps (**Show**), and a note on anything in the request it could not follow. A draft on tables not mapped yet says **Not Mapped Yet**; **Open in Editor** maps them as a dataset first. **Open in Editor** makes it a private report of yours and opens it in the editor; nothing is shared until you save and share it. **Try Again** asks once more; **Edit Prompt** puts your words back in the box.
   - **One question first**, when the prompt has two readings ("which period is last month?"), with the answers as buttons. Answering does not use another prompt.
   - **No field for this**, when no dataset holds what you asked for, with the closest prompts the catalogue can answer. The model never makes a field up; ask your platform team to map the data if you need it.
4. The line at the top right counts the month's prompts against your plan. Only a draft counts.
5. **Prompt History** lists your prompts of the last 90 days, newest first, with the report each became; older ones load as you scroll. **Use Again** puts one back in the box, the pencil renames its report (or its draft not opened yet), **Show** hides the list, and **Clear History…** takes your prompts off it after asking. Clearing does not give prompts back: the month's count and the workspace's records are unchanged.

Prompts and drafts are kept in the workspace for 90 days. Settings → AI Assistant switches AI off for the whole workspace, or allows only one of the providers.

**Your own key.** Under Settings → AI Assistant each provider shows whether it runs on the platform's key or on yours. **Use Your Own Key** (or **Add Your Own Key**, when the platform does not offer that provider) takes your API key from the provider's console and the two models to offer; the key is stored encrypted and never shown again, and **Check Connection** proves it works. Your own key wins over the platform's for that provider, and prompts on it are billed to you by the provider and not limited by your plan. **Use the Platform's Key** goes back.

### The four views

Any report switches between the views without losing its fields; the switch sits above the result, in the editor and in the viewer. The editor saves the choice; the viewer switches for a look.

| View | What it shows | What it needs |
|---|---|---|
| Grid | One row per record or group, sortable and resizable columns | Anything |
| Chart | Bars, lines or area: the first dimension along the bottom, a series per value of the dimension across (else one per measure), the legend to show and hide, Show the Numbers under it. **Stacked** and **100% Stacked**: the series on top of each other in one bar per category, as numbers or as shares. **Combo**: the first value as bars and the others as lines, a line in another format (such as a rate) read on a scale on the right. **Pie**: the first measure by the first dimension as shares, the total in the middle, more than eight slices folded into Other. **Funnel**: the rows in the report's order as stages, each with its share of the first and the drop from the one before | A dimension and a measure |
| Hierarchy | The dimensions in their order, each group folding over the next, a subtotal per group, Expand All and Collapse All | Two dimensions and a measure |
| Pivot | The dimension across as columns, one measure in the cells (a select when there are several), a total on both edges | A dimension down, one across, a measure |

Subtotals and totals come from the engine, so averages and distinct counts are exact, and a report longer than the page still totals everything. A comparison column is not added up into a subtotal.

**Calculated measures.** Some measures are made from others: Delivered Rate is Delivered ÷ Sent × 100, Average Order Value is Amount ÷ Orders. They sit among the measures with the one calculation **Calculated**, and the engine computes the formula in every view, subtotal and figure, so a rate of a group is the rate of its totals, never an average of rates. A rate with nothing to divide by reads as empty. Your platform team adds them to the platform datasets; a workspace administrator adds them to a mapped dataset under **Calculated Measures** on the mapping page (Report Databases chapter).

### The viewer

Opening a report runs it and shows the saved view. Above the rows: the view switch; under the title: what the run says (rows, time, freshness, version), **Refresh** and, once scheduled, the schedule line. The buttons:

- **Pages:** a grid with more than 20 rows shows them a page at a time. Under it: which rows are shown ("Rows 41–60 of 482"), **Rows per page** (20, 50 or 100, remembered in your browser), Previous, the page numbers and Next. Turning a page does not run the report again, and sorting a column sorts every row. Dashboard tiles and the editor's preview page the same way.
- **Kept results and Refresh:** a report on one of your Report Databases is kept for 15 minutes (other reports for a minute), so opening it again, or a dashboard with it, is instant. The line under the title then says **kept from 10:42**. **Refresh** runs it again now.
- **Why some reports take longer:** a report on your own database runs there, live. One that adds up a whole month of a large table (millions of rows) can take half a minute; one that lists the newest rows, or one campaign's day, is quick. A date filter on a day worked out from a date and time (such as DATE(SENT_AT)) is run on the date and time itself, so the database's index can help.

Component and Folder sit beside the title; every other action is in the **More actions** menu (the three dots), so the whole list fits on any screen. The menu is grouped: Explain and Edit; Share, Comments, Alerts, Schedule and Versions; the exports and Print-Ready PDF; Show the SQL and Embed on Another Site; Delete last.

| Action | What it does |
|---|---|
| Component | Lists the report under another component of the suite, or Automatic (its data's); it moves at once (owner or administrator). |
| Folder | Files the report in a folder, or in a new one (owner or administrator). |
| Versions | Every saved version with who, when and the note. **Open** shows one as it was, read-only, with Back to Current. **Restore** makes it the current version again, as a new version; nothing is lost. |
| Schedule | Emails the report on a schedule (below). |
| Explain | What This Means (free, by rules) and, where the AI is on, What the Rows Show (above). |
| Edit | The editor (owner or administrator). |
| Export CSV, Export Excel, Export PDF | The report as a file: CSV the rows; Excel the view with its totals first and the rows after, with number formats; PDF the view styled for paper. |
| Delete | Removes the report and its versions (owner or administrator). |

## Dashboards

A dashboard is a page of tiles, each tile a saved report in one of its views or one of its figures, on **one period** that applies to every tile.

- **Insights → Dashboards** starts with **Dashboard Templates**: **Start from Scratch**, then the ready-made dashboards of the Base Reports packs. A template opens a preview of what its pack adds; **Use This Template** opens Add Base Reports on that pack (a pack's dashboards come with the pack).
- **Your Dashboards** lists the rest with their tiles, owner, sharing and when they changed. Search, filter by **Owner**, **Shared** or **Favourites** (the filters stay in the address), and switch between a list and a grid. Each dashboard's **⋯** opens, edits, duplicates (a private copy), shares, stars or deletes it.
- **Create Dashboard** opens an empty one in the editor, which takes the whole window. The bar has the name and its saved state, **Close** (**Discard Changes** once something changed) and **Save**, which keeps the dashboard as a new version; leaving with changes asks first. The settings line has the period, the comparison, **Slicers**, **Theme**, **Who Can Open It** (only you, or the workspace), **Grid** or **Free Canvas**, **Phone Preview** and **Add**.
- **Add** opens a panel at the right: **Report**, **Text** or **Button**. For a report, search and pick one, then **Show It As** one of its views or one of its totalled columns as a **Figure**, a title and a **Size**. The tile goes after the others and is chosen, its toolbar showing; the panel stays open for the next.
- **Grid**: tiles flow left to right in their order, each at its size (Small to Full Width, and Tall). Its toolbar drags it to another place, sizes it, duplicates or removes it. See **Arrange a dashboard** above.
- **The period**: one chip with the quick choices (7 Days, 30 Days, 90 Days, This Month, Quarter…) and a calendar for a custom range, in the workspace's time zone, applied to every tile through its dataset's date. A report with its own date filter keeps it and the tile's line says **Own dates** and its dates. **Compare With Previous Period** adds the change to every figure.
- **Slicers**: in the editor, **Slicers → Add Slicer** picks a dimension the tiles' datasets share (Channel, Store, Campaign…), up to six. Viewing, each slicer is a chip after the period and Compare; open it, tick values and **Apply**, and every tile whose data has that field runs narrowed to them. A tile without the field says **Not sliced by** it. **Clear** takes the values off.
- **Cross-filtering**: click a bar, a slice of a pie, a stage of a funnel or a cell in a grid tile, and the other tiles narrow to that value. When the field is not one of the dashboard's slicers, a chip appears for it with a cross to take it off. Clicking the same value again clears it.
- Viewing a dashboard, you can switch the period, the comparison and the slicers for a look without saving; nothing of a selection is saved, and a PDF or a scheduled delivery is the dashboard as saved.
- **Tiles**: under its title, a line says what the tile reads: **Click a bar to filter the other tiles**, **Own dates** with its dates, or its report. Its **⋯** has **Open the Report**, **Show the Numbers** (its rows as a table), **Export CSV** and **Comment**. A tile refused because Insights is busy tries again by itself.
- The header has ‹ Dashboards, **Updated** with **Refresh** (runs every tile again), **Share** and **Edit**. The rest are in **More actions** (the three dots): Comments and Schedule; Export PDF and Export PowerPoint; Embed on Another Site; Delete last.
- **Export PDF** gives the dashboard with its tiles on its saved period; **Schedule** emails it as PDF.

## Scheduled delivery

Choose **Schedule** in **More actions** on a report or a dashboard:

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Every | Day, Weekday, Week (on a day) or Month (on a day of the month, 1 to 28). |
| At | The time, in the workspace's time zone. |
| Send as | Excel, CSV, PDF or PowerPoint for a report; a dashboard goes as PDF or PowerPoint. |
| To | Up to twenty addresses; Enter, a comma or a space adds one. |
| Subject | Optional. `{date}` becomes the sending date. Empty: the report's name and the date. |
| Note | Optional, at the top of the email. |

The email goes out through the workspace's own mail server (Settings → Email Server; a workspace without one cannot schedule), built at the moment of sending with the rights and masking of whoever scheduled it, the file attached. Under the title the schedule line shows what, when, to how many, the last sending and the next, with **Change** and **Pause**. **Send a Test Now**, on a saved schedule, sends the email to you at once. **Insights → Scheduled** lists every schedule you may see, with who it goes to, the last sending and the next; search, **Kind**, **Owner** and **Status** (Active, Paused, Failing) narrow it. **New Schedule** takes two steps: **What to Send** (a report or a dashboard), then **When and to Whom**, the fields above with a sentence that sums them up. Each schedule's **⋯** has **Edit Schedule** (it opens on the second step), Open, Pause or Resume and Delete. Three failures in a row pause a schedule; Resume starts it again. Alerts have their own tab there.

## Run Log

Insights → Run Log, for report administrators: every preview, run, export and delivery of the last 7 or 30 days, with the report and its version, the kind, who, the outcome, the rows and the time. The period's figures sit on top: runs, timed out, refused, failed and the median time. Search, **Period**, **Outcome** and **Person** narrow it (kept in the page's address). A run that did not go OK has an arrow beside its outcome: it opens a line saying why, with **Open the Report**. Twenty runs show at a time, or 50 or 100; **Export CSV** takes every run the filters show.

## How fresh is the data

The line under a report's title says it. **Live data**: the report reads the workspace's database as it is. **Data as of the last quarter hour**: the workspace reports on the analytics engine, which the platform team loads from the database every fifteen minutes; new sends show on the next load. The platform team decides which engine a workspace is on; the pages and the figures are the same either way.

## When something does not run

| Message | What it means |
|---|---|
| The report took longer than 15 seconds and was stopped | Narrow it with a filter or a shorter period. |
| X is personal data: building a report on it needs the report administration right | Ask an administrator to build that part, or to grant the right. |
| This workspace's reports are moving to a new engine | The platform team is switching the engine; try again shortly. |
| This workspace has no mail server yet | Set one up under Settings → Email Server before scheduling. |
| Insights is not part of this workspace | Ask the platform team to switch the component on. |

---

# Report Databases

**Where:** Settings → Report Databases, which needs the user administration right. A report author (Report Edit) can also connect a
database from Insights → New Report → **Connect a Database**; changing or removing a connection stays here.

## Step by step

**Connect a database** (workspace administrator)

1. Ask your database administrator for an account that holds SELECT and nothing else; the page shows the exact statements for MySQL, PostgreSQL and ClickHouse.
2. Open Settings → Report Databases and press **Connect a Database**. Give it a name, pick the engine (MySQL 5.7, MySQL 8.0, PostgreSQL or ClickHouse), enter the host, port, database, the schema for PostgreSQL, the user and the password, and choose SSL. **Find Databases** (on PostgreSQL **Find Schemas**) lists the databases the account can see with their table counts, so you pick one instead of typing it; it reads names only. A database or schema name may hold letters, digits, underscores and hyphens (such as txpostalprod-server-7); table and column names still need letters, digits and underscores.
3. Press **Test Connection**. Four facts come back: reached and the version, what the account can read, whether it is read-only, and the server's time zone. A ✕ on the read-only line names what the account holds beyond SELECT; have the account fixed before mapping anything. Where your platform does not require a read-only account (the global parameter **insights.report_db_read_only_required** set to No), the line reads **! Can write** instead and the connection is used as it is.
4. Press **Save**. The password is stored encrypted and never shown; leave it empty on a later edit to keep it.

**Set how reports treat a database** (workspace administrator)

1. Open Settings → Report Databases and press **Edit** on the database's card (or set it while you connect it).
2. Under **How Reports Treat This Database**, pick what kind of database it is:
   **Standard** for a reporting copy or a small database (the workspace's limits, usually 15 seconds and 4 reports at once);
   **Large or Slow** for big tables or a server behind a slow link (60 seconds, 2 at once);
   **Live Production Database** for the database the business runs on (60 seconds, 1 at once);
   or **Custom** to set the **Time Limit** (5 to 110 seconds) and **Reports at Once** (1 to 4) yourself.
3. Press **Save**. The next report uses it; nothing restarts. The card shows the choice and its two values.

A report that needs longer than the time limit is stopped, with a message that names the database and its limit. A report that finds the database busy with as many reports as it allows waits up to 10 seconds for its turn, then asks you to try again. Reading the database's list of tables and columns may take at least 60 seconds, or the time limit when that is longer.

**Map a dataset from a table** (workspace administrator)

1. On the database's card, press **Map a Dataset**. The list on the left holds every table and view by name, read live from the database when the page opens (one statement that opens no table; nothing about the database is stored). A large database opens on your **pinned** tables and on folded groups by name prefix (`sales_`, `inv_` ...); type part of a name to search them all, narrow the matches with the chips, and press **Show 50 More** for the next ones. The star pins a table to the top for everyone who maps datasets here. **Reload the List** reads the names again after the database changed. Pick a table or view: its columns and row estimate are read then, for that table only.
2. Name the dataset and describe it. Choose the **Row Key** (what one row is) and the **Time Field** (the date a period filter applies to).
3. For each column: tick it to keep it, give it a label, check its type and role, pick the calculations a measure allows, and set the sensitivity (**Personal** is masked for readers without the right, **Confidential** is hidden from everyone).
4. Watch the preview under the fields update with your labels, then press **Save Dataset**. It appears in New Report under **Your Databases**.

**Connect a database from New Report** (report author)

1. Open Insights → Reports → **New Report**. Under **Your Databases**, press **Connect a Database**. A panel opens over the page.
2. Fill in the same fields as above and press **Test Connection**. **Save and Continue** is offered once the test passes: the account read-only, or, where read-only is not required, able to write (the panel warns). Where read-only is required and the account can write, the panel says what it holds and asks for an account that can only read.
3. Pick a table or view (search narrows the list) and press **Create Report**. The table becomes a dataset with every readable column, labelled from its name, the first date as its time field; the report opens in the editor. A table mapped before keeps its dataset. Or press **write a query** to open the query window on the database.

**Add a calculated measure** (workspace administrator)

1. On the database's card press **Map a Dataset**, or **Edit Fields** beside an existing dataset.
2. Mark the numbers you need as **Measure**.
3. Under **Calculated Measures**, press **Add a Calculated Measure** and give it a label, such as Delivered Rate.
4. Pick the left side and its calculation, such as Sum of Delivered.
5. Pick the operation: **Divided by**, **Minus**, **Plus** or **Times**.
6. Pick the right side: another measure, or **A number…** to type a value.
7. Set the **Scale** (100 for a percentage) and the **Format**, and mark it Personal if it uses personal data. The formula is spelled out under the label as you build it.
8. Press **Save Dataset**, or **Save Fields** for a dataset made from a query. Report authors find it among the measures with the calculation **Calculated**.

**Join tables in a dataset**

1. On the mapping page, press **Add a Join**, or **Join to this** on a table's card in the diagram.
2. Choose what to join to (the base, or a table already joined), the table, and whether to keep every row (**Left**) or only the rows that match (**Inner**).
3. Say which columns are equal; the first pair is suggested from the names. Press **Check the Join** to see how many rows match and whether the join multiplies rows, then **Add**.
4. The joined table appears as a card with the key drawn to it; its columns are fields labelled with the table's name. Up to five joins; removing one removes the joins chained to it.

**Build a report from a query** (anyone who can build reports)

1. Open Insights → Reports → **New Report** and, under **Query a Database**, press the database.
2. **Visual**: press **From** beside a table on the left, add columns with their calculations and aliases, add conditions and sorts; the group by is set for you and the SQL shows underneath. **SQL**: write or paste the SELECT; click a table or column on the left to insert its name.
3. **Write It for Me**: describe the query in words, pick the model, and press it; the SQL lands in the window for you to read. It counts as one prompt.
4. Press **Run** to preview the rows. A second statement, a write word or a file function is refused with the reason before anything runs.
5. Name the report and press **Create Report**. The report opens in the editor on the query's result, where you group, sort, switch views, export and schedule as with any report.
6. Administrators reach the same window from **Query** on a database card; there it ends with **Use as Dataset**, and a dataset from a query shows **Edit Query** beside **Edit Fields**.

## What it is for

Insights builds reports on the data the platform already keeps. Report Databases adds your own: a MySQL 5.7, MySQL 8.0,
PostgreSQL or ClickHouse database you connect here (or from New Report), strictly to read. You pick the tables or views to report on and name their
columns, and those datasets appear in New Report and in Ask for a Report for your workspace alone. The editor, the four
views, exports, dashboards and scheduled delivery work on them like on any dataset.

The engine never writes. It only ever runs the SELECT statements it builds from a report, on a session it opens
read-only with the workspace's time limit, and by default it refuses an account that could write or change the schema
(the platform can lift that refusal with a global parameter; the sessions stay read-only either way). Nothing but
the columns you mapped is read.

## The account to create

Ask your database administrator for an account that holds SELECT and nothing else. The page shows the exact statements:

- MySQL: `CREATE USER 'cep_reader'@'%' IDENTIFIED BY '…'; GRANT SELECT ON your_db.* TO 'cep_reader'@'%';`
- PostgreSQL: a login role with CONNECT on the database, USAGE on the schema and SELECT on its tables.
- ClickHouse: `CREATE USER cep_reader IDENTIFIED BY '…' SETTINGS readonly = 2; GRANT SELECT ON your_db.* TO cep_reader;`
  An account whose profile is read-only (`readonly = 1` or `2`) passes whatever it was granted; otherwise it may hold
  SELECT and SHOW and nothing else. With `readonly = 1` the server also refuses the engine's time limit, so the server's
  own limits apply.

Grant SELECT on views alone to hide tables the engine should never see. The database must be reachable from CEP's
server; a private network address is fine when CEP's server sits on that network.

## Connecting a database

1. Press **Connect a Database**. Give it a name (what people see in New Report), pick the engine, and enter the host,
   port (3306 for MySQL, 5432 for PostgreSQL, 8443 for ClickHouse), database, user and password. For PostgreSQL, also the
   schema the tables live in (`public` unless told otherwise). SSL is Required by default; Preferred tries SSL first, Off
   is for a private network. ClickHouse is read over its HTTP interface: **HTTPS** on 8443, or **Off (plain HTTP)** on 8123
   on a private network; the port follows the choice.
2. **Test Connection** reaches the server and reports four facts: reached and the version, what the account can read,
   whether the account is read-only, and the server's time zone. A ✕ on the read-only line names what the account holds
   beyond SELECT; the connection can be saved but cannot be used until the account is fixed. Where the platform does not
   require a read-only account, it reads **! Can write** and the connection is used.
3. **Use for List Generation** (MySQL only): tick it for the workspace's CRM database. New Contact List then offers
   **Look Up in C360**, which reads people from it by their ID: name, mobile number, email, postal code, loyalty ID, time
   zone and whether they may be contacted (Contact Lists chapter). Read-only, 1,000 IDs a query. The card says *Used for
   list generation* when it is ticked.
4. **Save** keeps the connection and runs the test again. The password is stored encrypted and never shown; leave it
   empty on a later edit to keep it.

Each card shows the engine and version, host and database, the account with ✓ Read-only, ✕ Can write (refused) or ! Can write (allowed where read-only is not required), the datasets
mapped from it and the last test. **Test Connection** runs the check again; **Edit** changes the connection; **Remove**
is offered once its datasets are gone.

## Mapping a dataset

**Map a Dataset** on a card opens the mapping page.

1. The tables and views the account can read are on the left, views first, with their column counts and row counts.
   Search by name. A view is the safest source: it shows only what you put in it, already joined.
2. Pick one. Its columns fill the right side, each already typed from the column: a label made from the column's name,
   the type (Text, Number, Date, Date and time, Yes/No), the role (a number is a measure unless it looks like an id), the
   calculations a measure allows (Sum, Average, Min, Max, Count, Distinct) and a sensitivity. Untick a column to leave it
   out; it is then never read.
3. Name the dataset and describe it. Choose the **Row Key** (what one row is; optional) and the **Time Field** (the date a
   period filter applies to).
4. Mark personal columns **Personal**: they are masked for readers without the right, as in every dataset.
   **Confidential** hides a column from everyone.
5. The preview underneath shows the first rows with your labels applied, masked where you said so.
6. **Save Dataset**. It is in New Report under **Your Databases**, named with its database, and Ask for a Report knows
   it by label. **Edit Fields** in the table under the cards reopens it.

### Calculated measures

Under the columns, **Calculated Measures** makes a measure from the others: a rate, a share, a difference or a product.
**Add a Calculated Measure**, name it (Delivered Rate), pick the left side (a measure and its calculation, Sum of
Delivered), the operation (Divided by, Minus, Plus, Times), the right side (another measure, or **A number…**), a scale
(100 for a percentage) and a format. The formula reads under the label as you build it. Report authors get it as a
measure with the one calculation Calculated; the engine computes it in every view and total, so a group's rate is the
rate of its totals. A ratio with an empty denominator reads as empty, never as an error. Up to ten per dataset; one
formula may read another calculated measure.

## Joining tables

A dataset can read more than one table. The diagram above the fields shows the base table as a card with its columns;
**Add a Join** (or **Join to this** on a card) brings another table in:

1. Pick what to join to: the base, or a table already joined, which makes a chain such as sales → stores → regions.
2. Pick the table, and whether to keep every row of the table joined to (**Left**: a row with no match still shows, with
   the joined fields empty) or only the rows that match (**Inner**).
3. Say which columns are equal, one pair or more. The first pair is suggested from the names (store_id = id).
4. **Check the Join** before adding it: how many rows match, and a warning when the join multiplies rows, which would
   count the base's rows more than once in totals. It checks the first 10,000 rows of the base, never a whole table, and says so.

The joined table appears as a card with the key drawn to it, its columns ticked as fields and labelled with the table's
name ("Store Name", "Store City"). A key column is read for the join whether or not it is a field. Up to five joins;
removing a join removes the ones chained to it. The engine still writes one SELECT, read-only, under the same limits.

A dataset with reports built on it cannot be deleted until those reports are; a database with datasets cannot be removed
until they are.

## Query a Database: a report from a query

New Report offers **Query a Database** for every database your workspace has connected. Anyone who can build reports
can use it; the connection, its SELECT-only account and the limits are the administrator's.

- **Visual** builds the query from the tables and columns on the left: From (a table, then joins on a key), Columns
  with a calculation (Sum, Count, Count Distinct, Average, Min, Max) and an alias, Where conditions, Order by. Group
  by is set from the columns without a calculation. The SQL the builder writes shows underneath.
- **SQL** is the same window as text: write, paste or correct. Click a table or column on the left to put its name at
  the cursor. **Write It for Me** sends your request and the table and column names (never rows) to the AI assistant
  and puts the SQL in the window for you to read; it counts as one prompt. A query edited by hand stays in SQL; Start
  Over returns to Visual.
- **Run** shows the first rows with the time and the typed columns, under the workspace's limits.
- **Create Report** makes a dataset from the result (labels from the column names, numbers as measures) and opens the
  report in the editor, where you group, sort, switch views, export and schedule as with any report.

The window takes one SELECT and nothing else: a second statement, a write or schema word (INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE,
DROP, CREATE, ALTER, GRANT, SET, INTO …) or a file or system function is refused with the reason before anything runs.
The engine runs the query wrapped, `SELECT * FROM (your query) AS q` with the limit, on the read-only session; a query
that outgrows the time limit is stopped with advice to narrow it or put the heavy part in a view. A query never runs
against CEP's own database.

Under Settings → Report Databases, **Query** on a database card does the same for administrators and ends with **Use
as Dataset**, which opens the fields to name; a dataset from a query shows "From a query" with **Edit Query** beside
Edit Fields.

## When a report runs

The freshness line under a report on one of your datasets reads "live from <database>": the engine reads your database
at run time, under the same limits as any report (the statement time limit, the preview and run caps). A database that
does not answer shows "<database> did not answer" on the report; the Run Log records it like any run.

---

# AI Assistant

**Where:** Settings → General → AI Assistant.

## What this page is for

This page decides whether C360 may use AI in reports, which AI providers it may use, whose key pays for them, and whether a report's rows may be sent to the AI for a short reading. It is one switchboard for every AI feature of Insights:

- **Ask for a Report** on Insights → AI Reports, which turns a prompt into a draft report.
- **Change With AI** in the report editor, which changes the report you are editing from a prompt.
- **Write It for Me** in a Report Database's query window, which writes the SQL from a prompt.
- **What the Rows Show** in a report's Explain panel, and the **Written Summary** tile on a dashboard, which describe a report's rows in a few sentences.

Two providers are offered: Claude (Anthropic) and OpenAI. Each runs either on the platform's key, within your plan's monthly allowance, or on your workspace's own key, which your plan does not limit.

## Who can use it

| | |
|---|---|
| Permission | `user.admin` (workspace administration) to open the page, change the switches and add or remove keys. |
| Using the AI features | Ask for a Report, Change With AI and Write It for Me need `report.edit` or `report.admin`. What the Rows Show and the Written Summary tile are for anyone who can open the report or dashboard. |

## The fields and controls

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Use AI in Reports | The main switch. On by default. It cannot be switched on while no provider is in use (the page says so). | **On:** the AI features above are offered to the people allowed to use them, and **AI Reports** appears in the Insights menu. **Off:** none of them is offered; reports are built in the editor only, and no prompt or field name leaves C360. The provider switches and Explain This Report are greyed out while it is off. |
| Providers | One row per provider, with its state: **Provided by the Platform**, **Not Offered**, or, with your own key, **Not Checked Yet**, **Checked** (with the time) or **Check Failed** (with the provider's reason). The line under the name says which models it runs. | What people can pick at the bottom of the prompt box. |
| Allow (the switch beside a provider) | Shown when the platform offers the provider and you have no key of your own for it. On by default. | **Off:** that provider's models are no longer offered in this workspace. With every provider off, the AI features are not offered even with Use AI in Reports on. |
| Use Your Own Key / Add Your Own Key | Opens the key form. **Use Your Own Key** when the platform offers the provider; **Add Your Own Key** when it does not. | Once saved, the provider runs on your key from then on (see below). |
| Edit Key | Opens the key form for a key you already saved. | Change the models, or replace the key. |
| Check Connection | Asks the provider for the account's model list with your stored key, and looks for your two models in it. | Records the result and its time on the provider's row. A key that does not work shows the provider's message; a model name the account does not list is named, so you can correct it. Saving a new key clears the last check. |
| Use the Platform's Key / Remove | Deletes your own key, after asking. **Use the Platform's Key** when the platform offers the provider; **Remove** when it does not. | Prompts go through the platform's key again, within your plan; or, when the platform does not offer the provider, its models leave the picker. |
| Explain This Report | Shown once a provider is in use. On by default. Greyed out, with "Switched off for the platform by the platform team.", when the platform does not allow it. | **On:** What the Rows Show and the dashboard's Written Summary tile are offered. **Off:** both are refused; the free What This Means tab of the Explain panel stays. |
| AI Prompts This Month | The prompts counted this month, against your plan's allowance when it has one ("12 of 200"), or "No limit on this plan". | Nothing; a reading. It also says how long prompts and drafts are kept: 90 days. |

### The key form

| Field | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| API Key | The key from the provider's console (console.anthropic.com → API Keys, or platform.openai.com → API Keys). Required the first time; when a key is stored, leave it empty to keep it. | Stored encrypted and never shown again. The browser never sees it: every call goes from C360's server to the provider. |
| Model for Reports | The provider's model name, offered in the picker as "Best for reports". Filled in with the recommended model: `claude-sonnet-5-5` for Claude, `gpt-5` for OpenAI. Up to 80 characters: letters, digits, dots, dashes, underscores and colons. | The model the prompts run on when someone picks "Best for reports". |
| Fast Model | The second model, offered as "Fastest". Filled in with `claude-haiku-5-5` for Claude, `gpt-5-mini` for OpenAI. Same rules. | The model the prompts run on when someone picks "Fastest". |

Prompts on your own key are billed to you by the provider and are not limited by your plan.

## How to

**Switch AI off for the whole workspace.** Switch off **Use AI in Reports**. It saves at once (a tick confirms it). AI Reports leaves the Insights menu and the AI features are no longer offered; anything that still asks is answered "AI is switched off for this workspace (Settings → AI Assistant)."

**Use only one provider.** Leave **Use AI in Reports** on and switch off the provider you do not want. Its models leave the picker.

**Run on your own Claude key.** Press **Use Your Own Key** on the Claude row, paste the key, keep or change the two models, **Save**, then **Check Connection**. The row now says "Your own key" and prompts on it no longer count against the plan.

**Keep report rows away from the AI.** Switch off **Explain This Report**. Ask for a Report and Change With AI still work: they never send rows.

**Go back to the platform's key.** Press **Use the Platform's Key** on the provider's row and confirm.

## What happens behind the scenes

1. **What the AI receives.** Ask for a Report and Change With AI send your prompt and the names, types and calculations of the fields you may use (Change With AI also sends the report being edited), never customer rows. Write It for Me sends your request and the database's table and column names. What the Rows Show and the Written Summary tile send the report's name, its column names and up to 100 of the rows the person sees; columns hidden from that person as personal data are left out.
2. **Which key is used.** For each provider, your own key wins over the platform's whenever you have one, whatever the provider's Allow switch says. Otherwise the platform's key is used when the platform offers the provider and the switch is on.
3. **Which model is offered first.** The platform team's default model, when your workspace offers it; otherwise the first model in the list (Claude's model for reports when Claude is in use).
4. **What counts as a prompt.** Only results count: a draft from Ask for a Report, a report changed by Change With AI, a query from Write It for Me, an explanation from What the Rows Show or the Written Summary tile. A question back from the AI, an answer to it, and a failed call do not count. Only prompts on the platform's key count; prompts on your own key are never counted.
5. **When the allowance runs out.** Prompts on the platform's key are refused for the rest of the month, with a message saying how many were used. Prompts on your own key, and building reports by hand, carry on.
6. **What is kept.** Every prompt is recorded in the workspace for 90 days, with who asked, the model and the outcome, for your audit trail. Documents and images sent with a prompt are not kept.

## Things to know

- The platform team decides which providers the platform's key offers and whether Explain This Report is allowed at all. This page can narrow that, never widen it, except by adding your own key.
- Switching AI off does not touch the reports the AI made: they are ordinary reports and stay where they are.
- The What This Means tab of the Explain panel uses no AI and is there whatever this page says.
- The AI can be wrong. A draft is checked against your data before you see it, but read an explanation's figures before you act on them.
- Ask for a Report is described step by step in the Insights chapter; Write It for Me in the Report Databases chapter.

---

# Fiscal Year

**Where:** Settings → Insights → Fiscal Year.

## What this page is for

Many businesses do not run their year from January to December. This page tells C360 the month your fiscal year starts in, and how a fiscal year is named. Insights then groups, filters, compares and labels by your fiscal quarters and years, so a report reads "FY2027 Q3" the way your finance team does.

Until someone sets it, the fiscal year is the calendar year.

## Who can use it

| | |
|---|---|
| Permission | Anyone signed in can read it (reports need it). Changing it needs `user.admin` (workspace administration). |

## The fields and controls

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Starts In | The month the fiscal year starts in, January to December. Default: January. Saves as soon as you choose. | Where every fiscal year and fiscal quarter begins: the four quarters are the three-month blocks counted from this month. Any month but January also adds **by Fiscal Quarter** and **by Fiscal Year** to the ways a date can be grouped, and the **Fiscal Quarter** and **Fiscal Year** periods to dashboards. |
| Name a Fiscal Year By | **The Year It Ends In** (default) or **The Year It Starts In**. Greyed out while Starts In is January, when the fiscal year is the calendar year. Saves as soon as you choose. | Only the name. With April: April 2026 to March 2027 is **FY2027** by the year it ends in, **FY2026** by the year it starts in. The dates covered do not change. |
| The example line | A sentence under the fields: "FY2027 runs from April 2026 to March 2027; today is in FY2027 Q3.", or "Your fiscal year is the calendar year." | Nothing; it reads your choice back so you can check it. |

## What it changes in Insights

| Where | With the calendar year (January) | With another start month |
|---|---|---|
| Grouping a date in the editor | by Day, Week, Month, Quarter, Year. | Also **by Fiscal Quarter** and **by Fiscal Year**, labelled FY2027 Q1, FY2027. |
| Date filters | **in this fiscal year**, **in last fiscal year**, **in this fiscal quarter** and **in last fiscal quarter** are offered and cover calendar years and quarters. | The same filters cover your fiscal years and quarters. |
| Year to Date and Quarter to Date comparisons | Count from 1 January and from the calendar quarter's first day. | Count from the fiscal year's first day and from the fiscal quarter's first day. |
| Dashboard period | **This Year** (1 January to today); no fiscal quarter. | **Fiscal Quarter** and **Fiscal Year**: from the first day of the current fiscal quarter or year to today. |
| Excel and PDF exports, the drill path, the result's labels | Calendar labels. | A fiscal column is written as FY2027 Q1 or FY2027. |

## A worked example

Your fiscal year starts in **April**, named by **The Year It Ends In**, and today is 20 November 2026.

| Fiscal period | Dates |
|---|---|
| FY2027 | 1 April 2026 to 31 March 2027 |
| FY2027 Q1 | April to June 2026 |
| FY2027 Q2 | July to September 2026 |
| FY2027 Q3 (today) | October to December 2026 |
| FY2027 Q4 | January to March 2027 |

- A report filtered **in this fiscal quarter** reads 1 October to 31 December 2026; **in last fiscal quarter** reads 1 July to 30 September 2026.
- **in this fiscal year** reads 1 April 2026 to 31 March 2027; **in last fiscal year** reads 1 April 2025 to 31 March 2026.
- Revenue grouped **by Fiscal Quarter** shows rows FY2027 Q1, FY2027 Q2, FY2027 Q3. A sale on 15 February 2026 falls in FY2026 Q4.
- A **Year to Date** column on that report adds up from 1 April 2026.
- A dashboard set to **Fiscal Year** shows 1 April 2026 to today; its previous period, for a tile that compares, is the same number of days just before.

Switch **Name a Fiscal Year By** to **The Year It Starts In** and the same rows read FY2026 Q1, FY2026 Q2, FY2026 Q3; the dates stay the same.

## How to

**Set your fiscal year.** Open Settings → Fiscal Year, choose the month under **Starts In**, then choose how to **Name a Fiscal Year By**. Each choice saves at once; check the example line.

**Report by fiscal quarter.** In the report editor, drag a date into Rows and choose **by Fiscal Quarter**. To keep it to this quarter, add a filter on the same date and choose **in this fiscal quarter**.

## What happens behind the scenes

1. The setting is stored on the workspace. Reports keep "in this fiscal quarter", never the dates, so every run works the dates out again from the setting and today's date in the workspace's time zone.
2. Changing the start month changes every report and dashboard that uses a fiscal grouping, filter, comparison or period from its next run, including schedules and alerts. Results kept from before are not reused for the new year.
3. A fiscal quarter or year in a result is the date of its first day; C360 names it (FY2027 Q1) on screen and in exports with the workspace's naming.

## Things to know

- With January, the fiscal filters still work and simply mean the calendar year and quarter.
- Changing the start month after reports exist moves their fiscal figures; tell the people who read them.
- The fiscal year applies to Insights. Campaign schedules and Home do not use it.

---

# Report Access

**Where:** Settings → Insights → Report Access.

## What this page is for

Sharing decides which reports and dashboards a person can open. Report Access decides which **rows** they see inside them. A rule limits a person, or everyone holding a role, to some values of one field of a dataset: a store manager sees only their own stores, a regional lead only their region. The rule applies wherever that dataset is read for them, so it cannot be worked around by building another report.

People without a rule see every row of what they can open.

## Who can use it

| | |
|---|---|
| Permission | `report.admin` to see, add, change and remove rules. |
| Who a rule can name | Any active person of the workspace, or any role. A role reaches everyone holding it, including people given the role later. |

## The fields and controls

### The page

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Add a Rule | Opens the rule dialog. | |
| Who | The person (with their email address) or the role (with how many people hold it) the rule is for. A person or role that no longer exists reads "Removed". | |
| Dataset | The dataset the rule limits. | Only reads of this dataset are limited; other datasets are untouched. |
| Sees Only | The field and the values allowed ("Store: Lakeside Mall, Downtown"), with the rule's note under it. A red line says when the dataset no longer offers the field. | |
| Changed | When the rule was last saved. | |
| Edit | Opens the rule in the dialog, titled **Change a Rule**. | |
| Remove | Deletes the rule after asking ("They will see every row again."). | From the next run, the person or role sees every row of that dataset again, unless another rule still limits them. |

When there is no rule yet the page says so: everyone sees every row of the reports and dashboards they can open.

### The rule dialog (Add a Rule, Change a Rule)

| Field | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Who | Type part of a name, an email address or a role's name in **Find a person or a role**, then pick one from the list. Once picked it shows as a chip; **Change** clears it so you can pick someone else. Required. | Whose reads the rule limits. A rule on a role limits every holder of the role. |
| Dataset | The workspace's datasets. Required. Changing it empties Field. | Which dataset's reads are limited. |
| Field | The dataset's text, number and yes/no fields that group (dimensions), such as Store or Region. Required. Measures and date fields are not offered. | The field the values are checked against. |
| Sees Only | The allowed values, one per line or separated by commas, written exactly as the reports show them. From 1 to 500 values; repeats are dropped. A number field needs numbers, a yes/no field `true` or `false`. Required. | Only rows whose field holds one of these values are shown. |
| Note | Optional, up to 200 characters: why the rule exists, for whoever reads it later. | Shown under the values on the page. It does not change what anyone sees. |
| Add Rule / Save | Saves the rule. Greyed out until Who, Field and at least one value are given. | The rule applies from the person's next run. |

A person or role can have one rule per field of a dataset. To change the values, edit that rule; a second rule on the same field is refused with "That person or role already has a rule on this field; change it instead."

## How the rules combine

| Situation | What the person sees |
|---|---|
| A rule for the person and a rule for one of their roles, on the **same field** | The values of both rules together: Store is Lakeside Mall (their own rule) or Downtown (their role's rule). |
| Rules on **different fields** of the dataset | Every rule applies: Region is North **and** Store is Lakeside Mall. |
| No rule on the dataset | Every row. |
| A rule on a field the dataset no longer offers, or a value that does not fit the field's type | Nothing: the run is refused with a message asking them to have the rule changed. C360 never shows everything instead. |

## Where a rule applies

Every read of the dataset made for that person:

- Reports, in the viewer and the editor, with a note above the rows: "Your access shows only Store: Lakeside Mall, Downtown."
- Dashboard tiles, and the values offered by a dashboard's slicers.
- Exports and downloads of the report or dashboard.
- Scheduled deliveries and alerts, which are built with the rights of their owner: the owner's rules decide the rows every recipient receives and the value an alert watches.
- Linked measures read from another dataset, which carry that dataset's rules for the person.
- What the Rows Show (Explain): the AI reads only the rows the person sees.
- **Show the SQL**, which lists the rules it added ("Your row access rules are in it: …").

Not limited: the query window of a workspace's own Report Database, which runs SQL as written. Give that window only to people who may see all of the database.

## How to

**Limit a store manager to their stores.** Add a Rule → find the person → Dataset: the sales dataset → Field: Store → Sees Only: their store names, one per line → Note: "Store manager, Lakeside and Downtown" → **Add Rule**. Open one of their reports as a check: the note above the rows names the stores.

**Limit a whole team.** Make the rule on their role. Everyone who holds the role, now or later, is limited.

**Lift a limit for one person while their role keeps it.** Rules only narrow. Give the person a role without the rule instead.

## What happens behind the scenes

1. At every run C360 reads the person's own rules and their roles' rules for the dataset, and adds them to the report's filters before the query runs. The rules come first, before the report's own filters, parameters and the result cache, so a result kept for someone else is never shown to them.
2. A change to a rule is saved with a version number; saving over someone else's newer change is refused ("The rule changed since you opened it") and asks you to reload.

## Things to know

- Report Access limits rows, not reports. Who can open a report or dashboard is set by sharing (Share on the report or dashboard).
- Write the values as the reports show them, including capitals and spaces; a value that matches nothing shows nothing.
- Report administrators are limited by a rule that names them, like anyone else.
- Masking personal data is separate: a person without the right to see personal data sees it masked, whatever their rules.

---

# Governance

**Where:** Settings → Engagement → Audience → Governance. Home's Governance card opens it with **Open Governance**.

## What this page is for

How often marketing may reach one person, and what those rules did in the last 30 days. Every marketing send is checked against the rules just before it goes out: a person who has reached a cap is skipped by that send and counted as **Frequency Capped** on the campaign's results. Transactional messages and e-receipts are never capped and never count towards a cap.

Some caps are set by the platform team for your workspace (sometimes for several workspaces at once). They are shown locked, marked **Platform**. You add caps of your own beside them. Every cap applies at the same time, so a cap of your own can only make sending stricter than the platform's, never looser.

## The standard caps

Every workspace starts with the industry-standard set:

| Set By | Cap | Can You Change It |
|---|---|---|
| Platform | 1 marketing email per day, 1 text per day, 1 WhatsApp message per day | No: the platform's limit for every workspace, so nobody receives a burst of messages. |
| This Workspace | 3 emails per 7 days, 4 texts per 30 days, 2 WhatsApp messages per 7 days, 5 messages per 7 days across all channels | Yes: edit, switch off or remove any of them, and add more. |

A workspace with no caps of its own sees **Use the Recommended Caps**, which adds the workspace set in one go.

## Gap Between Messages

A gap keeps marketing of one channel apart: a person who received a marketing email in the last 16 hours is skipped by the next email send, like Klaviyo's Smart Sending. Each channel (Email, Text, WhatsApp) has one gap of your own, switched on or off, from 1 to 168 hours (7 days). It starts at the recommended hours: 16 for email, 24 for texts and WhatsApp.

A gap is checked with the caps, and a person it skips is counted as **Frequency Capped** too. The platform team may also set a gap for your workspace; it is shown locked under its channel, and both apply.

A cap of one message per day already keeps 24 hours between messages, so a gap of 24 hours or less adds nothing while such a cap applies. The page says so under the gap. The platform's standard caps are 1 per day on every channel, so a gap of your own matters when it is longer than 24 hours, or when that cap is switched off.

## Send Windows and Quiet Hours

A send window says when marketing of one channel may go out: from one time to another, on chosen days, in the workspace's time zone. Quiet hours are the same rule seen from the other side: a window of 08:00 to 21:00 means quiet hours of 21:00 to 08:00.

A marketing send due outside its window is not skipped: it **waits** and starts when the window opens. A campaign scheduled for Saturday at 22:00 with an email window of 07:00 to 21:00 goes out on Sunday at 07:00 (or on Monday at 07:00 if Sunday is not one of the window's days). Transactional messages, e-receipts and test sends to testers never wait.

Each channel (Email, Text, WhatsApp) has one window of your own, switched on or off. The platform team may set a window for your workspace too; it is shown locked under its channel. Every window applies, so a send goes out only when it is inside all of them: your own can only narrow the platform's. A window whose hours never meet the platform's is refused, since nothing could ever go out.

A window can run past midnight (22:00 to 02:00); it belongs to the day it opens. Until a workspace has a text window, the platform's older quiet hours for texts still apply and are shown under Text.

A campaign set to **Send at Each Recipient's Local Time** is checked against its window when its send starts, in the workspace's time zone; each person's message then goes at their own local time.

## Blackout Dates

A blackout date is a day with no marketing at all, on any channel: Christmas Day, a national day of mourning, the day of a system change. It is read in the workspace's time zone, from midnight to midnight.

A marketing send due on a blackout date is not skipped: it **waits** for the next day that is not blacked out, and then for that channel's send window. With an email window of 07:00 to 21:00 and blackout dates on 25 and 26 December, a campaign scheduled for Christmas morning goes out on 27 December at 07:00. Transactional messages, e-receipts and test sends to testers never wait.

A date can be one day or a run of up to 31 days (24 December to 2 January), once or **Every Year** on the same dates. Holidays that move from year to year, such as Thanksgiving, Easter or Eid, are added for each year. The platform team may set dates for your workspace too; they are listed locked, marked **Platform**, and every date applies.

The list shows the soonest dates first. A one-off date that has passed stays listed, marked **Passed**, until you remove it. A date that is on now is marked **Today**.

## Unengaged Contacts

Mailbox providers such as Gmail and Outlook decide between the inbox and the spam folder partly by how many recipients ignore a sender. Mailing people who stopped reading long ago pulls every other message towards spam. **Unengaged Contacts** leaves those people out of marketing email.

A person is left out when all three are true:

1. Their first marketing email from CEP went out at least **180 days** ago, so CEP has watched them for the whole period.
2. They were sent at least **5** marketing emails in the last 180 days.
3. In those 180 days they made no **email click**, **text link click**, **purchase** (an order or an e-receipt) or **preference-page visit**.

Opens are not counted: Apple Mail opens every message on its own, so an open says nothing about the reader. Clicks made by security scanners are not counted either. Nobody is unsubscribed or added to Suppressions: the next click or purchase brings the person back on the next send. Texts, transactional email and e-receipts are never affected.

The card shows what the rule does today: how many of your email contacts it leaves out, how many engaged in the period, and how many are **too new to judge** (mailed for less than the period, or sent fewer emails than the minimum). It also shows the last 30 days: recipients left out, and people who came back after a click or purchase. **Download the People** gives a CSV of everyone left out, to build a win-back list from.

**A new install.** CEP judges a person only after the period has passed since their first marketing email, so for the first 180 days nobody is left out. The card says when it starts: "Starts 18 May 2027" for a workspace whose first marketing email went out on 19 November 2026. People added later each get their own 180 days.

**A win-back campaign** can reach these people: turn on **Include Unengaged Contacts** in the campaign's Review & Publish → When and How It Goes Out. It is off by default and recorded in the activity log when the campaign is published.

The platform team may set a longest allowed, shown locked on the card ("Platform: at most 270 days"). Your own rule may only be stricter: fewer days, or fewer emails.

## Who can use it

| | |
|---|---|
| Open the page | Workspace administrators (`user.admin`, as for every Settings page). |
| Add, change or remove a cap | `governance.edit` on a role. Without it the page is read-only. |

## The fields and controls

| Control | What it is | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| Last 30 Days · Recipients Checked | One for each person on each campaign send in the last 30 days, kept or skipped for any reason: a person on three sends counts three times. | Nothing: a figure. |
| Last 30 Days · Sent Within the Rules | The share of those recipients who were sent the message. | Nothing: a figure. A low share means many people were left out, by caps, consent, opt-outs or blocked addresses. |
| Last 30 Days · Skipped by a Cap or Gap | Recipients a cap or a gap held back. | Nothing: a figure. Each one is counted as Frequency Capped on the campaign's Recipients tab. |
| Frequency Caps table | Every cap that applies to this workspace: Channel, Most (for example *3 emails per 7 days*), Set By (**Platform** or **This Workspace**), In Use. | Each cap is checked on every marketing send of its channel. |
| Channel | Email, Text (SMS and MMS together), WhatsApp, or All Channels. | Which sent messages are counted. All Channels counts every email, text and WhatsApp message together; it applies to every marketing send. |
| Most | 1 to 1,000. | The number of marketing messages one person may have received in the period before the next one is skipped for them. |
| Days | 1 to 365. A rolling period ending now. | How far back the messages are counted. |
| In Use | A switch on each of your own caps; *On* or *Off* for the platform's. | Off: the cap stays listed but is not checked. |
| Add Cap | Opens the cap form. | Adds a cap of your own. It applies from the next send. |
| Use the Recommended Caps | Shown while the workspace has no caps of its own. | Adds the standard workspace caps above for every channel not capped yet. Each is then yours to change. |
| Gap Between Messages · Hours | 1 to 168, one box per channel. Saved when you leave the box or press Enter. | How long after a marketing message of that channel the next one is skipped for that person. |
| Gap Between Messages · Switch | On or off, per channel. | On: the gap is checked from the next send. Off: it is kept with its hours but not checked. |
| Edit | On your own caps. | Changes the cap from the next send; sends already built are not rebuilt. |
| Remove | On your own caps; asks first. | The cap no longer applies. |
| Locked | On the platform's caps. | The platform team changes or removes these in the back-office; you cannot. |
| Send Windows · From, To | Times in the workspace's time zone, saved when you leave the box. To earlier than From runs past midnight. | When marketing of the channel may start and stop going out. |
| Send Windows · Days | Mon to Sun; at least one. Saved on each click. | The days the window opens. |
| Send Windows · Switch | On or off, per channel. It starts at the recommended hours: email 07:00 to 21:00, texts and WhatsApp 09:00 to 20:00, every day. | On: sends of the channel wait for the window from the next send. Off: kept with its hours but not checked. |
| Send Windows · Bar | The 24 hours, with the window marked. | Nothing: a picture of the hours. |
